  - id: J1dd8d9c8b2265f58
    okf_path: OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3.md
    okf_sha256: b973183de77a23a5c4e6f7df14a6bfe6e19a3add3e80a6dc1db238991aff8dd3
    heading: Clause 43.0 — Noise
    source_document: Vol3DLNP1.pdf
    source_sha256: 1ee3fc8dfd12f659f34446b0a6db74e1e1cdd4d593ca91b2a48d59bf1ef97d52
    pdf_pages:
    - 68
    sheet_cells: ''
    json_path: parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json
    json_sha256: dc172436c564fe7d5a449f7f786e6863b16e68ebd59020d555002ab6fc04758d
    json_pointer: /clauses/2/fragments/2
    json_record_id: null
    source_review_status: pending_manual_review
    quote: "{\n  \"pdf_page\": 68,\n  \"text\": \"43.2.10  The Engineer shall monitor Contractor’s performance of\
      \ tasks specified, and  will\\n          inspect necessary records, reports and procedures related to the\
      \ control of noise.\\n\\n43.2.11  Impact monitoring shall be carried out at noise sensitive receptor locations\
      \ within 200\\n           feet of the construction site once each week and after a change in construction\\\
      n            activity. Construction noise measurements shall coincide with daytime and nighttime\\n      \
      \    periods of maximum noise generating construction activities.\\n\\n43.2.12  Noise Monitoring data will\
      \ be submitted in a Noise Measurement Report Form. It will\\n          contain the type of measurement, duration\
      \ of measurement, distance of monitoring\\n         from construction site, and construction equipment working\
      \ during monitoring period.\\n\\n43.2.13   appropriate  parameter  for measuring  construction  noise  impacts\
      \  shall be  the\\n          equivalent A-weighted sound pressure level (Leq) measured in decibels (dB). The\
      \ two\\n            statistical sound levels L10 and L90; the level exceeded for 10 and 90 percent of the\
      \ time\\n           respectively, shall also be recorded during monitoring. The L90 may be considered as\\\
      n          the ambient level into which the L10 as average peak level intrudes. The Lmax, Leq, L10\\n    \
      \    and L90 values will be reported in the noise measurement form along with allowable\\n         noise limit.\
      \ The duration of monitoring shall be for a minimum of 30 minutes.\\n\\n43.2..14  In no case  shall the Contractor\
      \ expose the public  to construction noise levels\\n         exceeding 90dBA (slow) or to impulsive noise\
      \ levels with a peak sound pressure level\\n         exceeding 140dB as measured on an impulse sound level\
      \ meter.\\n\\n43.2.15   Limit for construction noise is based on the existing ambient noise levels in areas\\\
      n          adjoining the construction sites.\\n\\n43.2.16 The noise levels emanating from any source during\
      \ construction, shall not exceed 5\\n         dB(A) or more above existing ambient pre-construction noise\
      \ levels when measured at\\n        a point outside the premises of the location of source. The same may be\
      \ varied from\\n         time to time by and at the sole discretion of the Engineer.\\n43.2.17  Where there\
      \ are no ambient noise measurements, the construction activities shall be\\n           limited to levels measured\
      \ at a distance of 200 feet from the construction limits or at\\n          the nearest affected building,\
      \ whichever is closer, as given in Table-2.\\n                                 TABLE-2\\n                \
      \  ALLOWABLE CONSTRUCTION NOISE\\n     LAND USE               MAXIMUM NOISE LEVELS- Lmax dB (A)\\n       \
      \                                     Day Time Night Time\\n          Residential                      75\
      \             65\\n          At all Times\\n          Commercial                                   85\\n \
      \            Industrial                                     90\\n\\n\\n43.2.18   At the surface of the construction\
      \ site during night time hours, the Contractor shall use\\n          only equipment that operating under full\
      \ load meets the noise limits specified in Table-\\n           3, if a sensitive receptor would be affected.\\\
      n                             TABLE-3\\n NOISE EMISSION LIMITS FOR CONSTRUCTION EQUIPMENT USED DURING NIGHTTIME\\\
      n              HOURS; MEASURED AT 50 FEET FROM CONSTRUCTION EQUIPMENT*\\n Equipment Category             \
      \                        Lmax Level dB(A)\\n\\n Backhoe                                                80\\\
      n Bar Bender                                              75\\n Chain Saw                                \
      \              81\\n Compactor                                              80\\n Compressor             \
      \                                80\\n Concrete Mixer                                           85\\n Concrete\
      \ Pump                                           82\\n Crane                                             \
      \     85\\n Dozer                                                  85\\n Front End Loader                \
      \                         80\\n Generator                                               82\\n Gradall    \
      \                                             85\\n Grader                                               \
      \   85\\n Paver                                                  85\"\n}"
    page_basis: Parsed record provenance; not independently PDF-verified

  - id: J45fcc9b04b07ae52
    okf_path: OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3.md
    okf_sha256: b973183de77a23a5c4e6f7df14a6bfe6e19a3add3e80a6dc1db238991aff8dd3
    heading: Clause 43.0 — Noise
    source_document: Vol3DLNP1.pdf
    source_sha256: 1ee3fc8dfd12f659f34446b0a6db74e1e1cdd4d593ca91b2a48d59bf1ef97d52
    pdf_pages:
    - 69
    sheet_cells: ''
    json_path: parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json
    json_sha256: dc172436c564fe7d5a449f7f786e6863b16e68ebd59020d555002ab6fc04758d
    json_pointer: /clauses/2/fragments/3
    json_record_id: null

    quote: "{\n  \"pdf_page\": 69,\n  \"text\": \"Pneumatic Tools                                          85\\\
      n          Scraper                                                 85\\n            Tractor              \
      \                                    84\\n\\n\\n         Noise emission limits apply to equipment used at\
      \ surface of the construction site during Night\\n                    time hours of 9 p.m. to 6 a.m.\\n\\\
      n      43.2.19     The adjustments for close in equipment noise measurement shall\\n                 be made\
      \ in accordance with Table-4.\\n\\n                                    TABLE – 4\\n              ADJUSTMENTS\
      \ FOR CLOSE-IN EQUIPMENT NOISE MEASUREMENTS\\n                    (Measurement Values to be subtracted from\
      \ Measured Sound)\\n         Distance (Feet)                      Level to Estimate Sound Level at 50 Feet\
      \ dB (A)\\n              19-21                                               8\\n              22-23     \
      \                                          7\\n              24-26                                       \
      \        6\\n              27-29                                               5\\n              30-33   \
      \                                            4\\n              34-37                                     \
      \          3\\n              38-42                                               2\\n              43-47 \
      \                                              1\\n              48-50                                   \
      \            0\\n\\n                                       TABLE- 5.\\n                          CONSTRUCTION\
      \ VIBRATION LIMITS\\n                             VIBRATION TYPE AND PERMISSIBL\\n       AGGREGATE DURATION\
      \            LIMIT\\n           Sustained (1 hr/day)                               0.01 in/sec (80 VdB re\
      \ 10-6 in/sec)\\n           Transient (<1 hr/day)                               0.03 in/sec (90 VdB re 10-6\
      \ in/sec)\\n           Transient (<10 min/day)                            0.10 in/sec (100 VdB re 10-6 in/sec)\\\
      n\\n\\n          43.2.20  When  Diesel Generator (DG) Sets  are used  for  operation  of equipment and\\n\
      \                   machinery, then ‘Standards and Guidelines  for  control  of Noise  Pollution from\\n \
      \                   Stationery DG Sets’, under Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 shall apply.\\n        \
      \ 43.2.21  Should the impact monitoring record noise levels which are:\\n                         indicative\
      \ of a deteriorating situation such that closer monitoring is reasonably\\n                            indicated,\
      \ or\\n                        when in the opinion of the Engineer additional measurements are required in\\\
      n                        view of deteriorating noise environment, then, the Engineer may require the\\n  \
      \                       Contractor to increase the frequency of impact monitoring at any one or more of\\\
      n                          the monitoring stations until the results indicate an improving and acceptable\\\
      n                             level of noise.\\n\\n        43.2..22    The Contractor  shall submit a copy\
      \  of monitoring  results. The  results should\\n                    represent  a statistical evaluation of\
      \ data for evaluation of trends and comparison\\n                      with noise emission standards.\\n\\\
      n        43.2.23   Where the Engineer determines that the recorded Noise level is significantly\\n       \
      \             greater than the acceptable levels, the Engineer may direct the Contractor to\\n           \
      \        take effective remedial measures including, but not limited to, reviewing noise\\n              \
      \    sources and modifying working procedures.\\n\\n         43.2.23    The Contractor shall inform the Engineer\
      \ of all steps taken to investigate cause of\\n                  exceedance and immediate action taken to\
      \ avoid further exceedance through\\n                       written reports and proposals for action under\
      \ an Event Contingency Plan.\\n\\n          43.3       Control Requirements\\n\\n\\n          43.3.1     Construction\
      \ material should be operated and transported in such a manner as not to\\n                     create unnecessary\
      \ noise as outlined below:\\n\\n\\n                                 i)    Perform Work within the procedures\
      \ outlined herein and comply with applicable\\n                        codes,  regulations, and  standards\
      \  established by  the  Central and  State\\n                     Government and their agencies.\"\n}"

- kind: summary
  text: The Safety, Health and Environment Manual Part III establishes mandatory contractor requirements regarding
    physical fitness, medical examinations, occupational health centers, ambulance services, first aid, HIV/AIDS
    prevention, vector control, noise, ventilation, illumination, radiation, and worker welfare facilities.
  evidence_ids:

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"page_rect\": [\n\
      \    0.0,\n    0.0,\n    595.219970703125,\n    842.0\n  ],\n  \"pdf_page\": 64,\n  \"review_status\": \"\
      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n  \
      \                                                        248\\n\\n               PART – III: OCCUPATIONAL\
      \ HEALTH AND WELFARE\\n\\n\\n41.0     Physical fitness of workmen\\n\\n\\n41.1    The contractor shall ensure\
      \ that his employees/workmen subject themselves to such\\n         medical examination as required under the\
      \ law or under the contract provision and\\n        keep a record of the same.\\n\\n\\n41.2    The contractor\
      \ shall not permit any employee/workmen to enter the work area under\\n          the influence of alcohol\
      \ or any drugs.\\n\\n\\n42.0     Medical Facilities\\n\\n\\n42.1     Medical Examination\\n\\n\\n42.1.1  \
      \ The contractor shall arrange a medical examination of all his employees including his\\n          sub-contractor\
      \ employees employed as drivers, operators of  lifting appliances and\\n          transport equipment before\
      \ employing, after illness or injury,  if  it appears that the\\n           illness or injury might have affected\
      \ his fitness and, thereafter, once in every two years\\n        up to the age of 40 and once in a year, thereafter.\\\
      n\\n\\n                i)    The Contractor shall maintain the confidential records of medical examination\
      \ or\\n                the physician authorized by the Employer.\\n\\n                  ii)   No building\
      \ or other construction worker is charged for the medical examination\\n             and the cost of such\
      \ examination is borne by contractor employing such building\\n               worker.\\n\\n              \
      \    iii)   The medical examination shall include: -\\n\\n\\n                a)     Full medical and occupational\
      \ history.\\n\\n                b)     Clinical examination with particular reference to\\n\\n           \
      \                         i)    General Physique;\\n\\n                                       ii)     Vision:-\
      \ Total visual performance using standard orthorator  like\\n                         Titmus  Vision  Tester\
      \  should be  estimated and  suitability  for\\n                         placement  ascertained  in accordance\
      \  with  the  prescribed  job\\n                           standards.\\n\\n                              \
      \          iii)    Hearing:  - Persons with normal must be able to hear a forced\\n                      \
      \    whisper at twenty-four feet. Persons using hearing aids must be\\n                           able to\
      \ hear a warning shout under noisy working conditions.\\n\\n                             iv)    Breathing:-\
      \ Peak flow rate using standard peak flow meter and the\\n                        average peak flow rate determined\
      \ out of these readings of the test\\n                          performed.  The  results  recorded  at  pre-placement\
      \  medical\\n                          examination could be used as a standard for the same individual at\\\
      n                           the same altitude for reference during subsequent examination.\\n\\n         \
      \               v)   Upper Limbs: - Adequate arm function and grip\\n\\n                             vi) \
      \   Spine: - Adequately flexible for the job concerned.\\n\\n                               vii)   Lower Limbs:\
      \ - Adequate leg and foot concerned.\\n\\n                                 viii)   General: - Mental alertness\
      \ and stability with good eye, hand and\\n                              foot coordination.\\n\\n         \
      \        c)   Any other tests which the examining doctor considers necessary\\n\\n\\n42.1.2.     If the contractor\
      \ fails to get the medical examination conducted as mentioned above,\\n          the employer will have the\
      \ right to get the same conducted by through an agency with\\n           intimation to the contractor and\
      \ deduct the cost and overhead charges.\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n\\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.           \
      \   September 2026     Section Page 60 of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n       \
      \ 47.039981842041016,\n        15.768376350402832,\n        561.0993041992188,\n        24.210372924804688\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 0,\n      \"text\": \"                                                \
      \                                                                                   Safety Health & Environment\
      \ (SHE) Manual \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.4399642944336,\n        26.57955551147461,\n\

      : \"PART – III: OCCUPATIONAL HEALTH AND WELFARE \\n \\n41.0  \\nPhysical fitness of workmen \\n \\n41.1  \\\
      nThe contractor shall ensure that his employees/workmen subject themselves to such \\nmedical examination\
      \ as required under the law or under the contract provision and \\nkeep a record of the same. \\n \\n41.2\
      \  \\nThe contractor shall not permit any employee/workmen to enter the work area under \\nthe influence of\
      \ alcohol or any drugs. \\n \\n42.0 \\nMedical Facilities \\n \\n42.1 \\nMedical Examination \\n \\n42.1.1\
      \  \\nThe contractor shall arrange a medical examination of all his employees including his \\nsub-contractor\
      \ employees employed as drivers, operators of lifting appliances and \\ntransport equipment before employing,\
      \ after illness or injury, if it appears that the \\nillness or injury might have affected his fitness and,\
      \ thereafter, once in every two years \\nup to the age of 40 and once in a year, thereafter.  \\n \\n\"\n\
      \    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        127.4378433227539,\n        336.0597839355469,\n        512.7656860351562,\n\
      \        423.5321350097656\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 5,\n      \"text\": \"i) \\nThe Contractor shall\
      \ maintain the confidential records of medical examination or \\nthe physician authorized by the Employer.\
      \ \\nii) \\nNo building or other construction worker is charged for the medical examination \\nand the cost\
      \ of such examination is borne by contractor employing such building \\nworker. \\niii) \\nThe medical examination\
      \ shall include: - \\n  \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.44039154052734,\n        428.5796813964844,\n\
      \        517.2083740234375,\n        778.971923828125\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 6,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"a) \\nFull medical and occupational history. \\nb) \\nClinical examination with particular reference to\
      \ \\ni) \\nGeneral Physique; \\nii) \\nVision:- Total visual performance using standard orthorator like \\\
      nTitmus Vision Tester should be estimated and suitability for \\nplacement ascertained in accordance with\
      \ the prescribed job \\nstandards. \\niii) \\nHearing: - Persons with normal must be able to hear a forced\
      \ \\nwhisper at twenty-four feet. Persons using hearing aids must be \\nable to hear a warning shout under\
      \ noisy working conditions. \\niv) \\nBreathing:- Peak flow rate using standard peak flow meter and the \\\
      naverage peak flow rate determined out of these readings of the test \\nperformed. The results recorded at\
      \ pre-placement medical \\nexamination could be used as a standard for the same individual at \\nthe same\
      \ altitude for reference during subsequent examination. \\nv) \\nUpper Limbs: - Adequate arm function and\
      \ grip \\nvi) \\nSpine: - Adequately flexible for the job concerned. \\nvii) \\nLower Limbs: - Adequate leg\
      \ and foot concerned. \\nviii) \\nGeneral: - Mental alertness and stability with good eye, hand and \\nfoot\
      \ coordination. \\nc) \\nAny other tests which the examining doctor considers necessary \\n \\n42.1.2. \\\
      nIf the contractor fails to get the medical examination conducted as mentioned above, \\nthe employer will\
      \ have the right to get the same conducted by through an agency with \\nintimation to the contractor and deduct\
      \ the cost and overhead charges. \\n \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        546.0439453125,\n\

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"page_rect\": [\n\
      \    0.0,\n    0.0,\n    595.219970703125,\n    842.0\n  ],\n  \"pdf_page\": 65,\n  \"review_status\": \"\
      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n249\\\
      n          42.2     Occupational Health Centre\\n\\n          42.2.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction\
      \ site an occupational health centre,\\n                 mobile or static is provided and maintained in good\
      \ order. Services and facilities\\n                as per the scale lay down in Schedule X of BOCWR. A construction\
      \ medical\\n                     officer appointed in an occupational health centre, possess the qualification\
      \ as\\n                     laid down in Schedule XI of BOCWR.\\n\\n\\n          42.3    Ambulance van and\
      \ room\\n\\n\\n          42.3.1   The contractor  shall ensure  at a construction  site of a building or other\\\
      n                  construction work that an ambulance van and room are provided at such\\n              \
      \    construction site or an arrangement is made with a nearby hospital for providing\\n                such\
      \ ambulance van for transportation of serious cases of accident or sickness\\n                   of workers\
      \  to  hospital promptly and such ambulance van and room are\\n                 maintained in good repair\
      \ and is equipped with standard facilities specified in\\n                Schedule IV and Schedule V of BOCWR.\\\
      n\\n\\n          42.4      First-aid boxes\\n\\n\\n          42.4.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction\
      \ site one First-aid box for 100 workers\\n                   provided and maintained for providing First-aid\
      \ to the building workers. Every First-aid\\n                 box  is  distinctly marked  “First-aid” and\
      \  is equipped with the  articles specified  in\\n                 Schedule III of BOCWR.\\n\\n\\n       \
      \   42.5     HIV/ AIDS prevention and control\\n\\n\\n          42.5.1   The contractor shall adopt the Employer’s\
      \ Policy on “HIV  / AIDS Prevention and\\n                    Control for Workmen Engaged by Contractors”\
      \ and the copy of the policy is given in\\n                Appendix No.: 4.\\n\\n          42.5.2   The Employer\
      \ will engage a professional agency for implementing the guidelines laid\\n               down in the policy\
      \ and communicate to the contractor.\\n\\n          42.5.3   The Contractor shall extend necessary support\
      \ to the appointed agency by deputing\\n                    the workmen to attend the awareness creation programmes.\\\
      n\\n          42.5.4   The contractor shall also extend necessary organizational support to the appointed\\\
      n                 agency  for the  effective implementation  of the Employers’ workplace  policy on\\n   \
      \              HIV/AIDS for workmen of the Contractors.\\n\\n          42.5.5   As laid down in the policy\
      \ the contractor shall identify peer educators (1 for every 100\\n                   workers) and refer them\
      \ for professional training to the Employers’ appointed agency\\n                       for the purpose.\\\
      n\\n          42.5.6   The peer educators on completion of the training shall serve as the focal point\\n\
      \                    for any information, education and awareness campaign among the workmen\\n          \
      \       throughout the contract period.\\n\\n          42.5.7   The peer educators will be paid a monthly\
      \ honorarium as fixed by the Employer for\\n                   rendering his services in addition to his regular\
      \ duty.\\n\\n          42.5.8   The total number of peer educators (1 for 100 workers) shall always be maintained\
      \ by\\n                    the contractor.\\n\\n          42.5.9    In case if these peer educators leave\
      \ the contractor by creating vacancy, then\\n                  the contractor at his own expense train the\
      \ new replacement peer educator from\\n                  the Employers’ appointed agency for the purpose.\\\
      n          42.5.10    It is suggested to the contractor that due care should be taken to select the peer\\\
      n                   educators from among the group of workmen so that they remain with the contractor\\n \
      \                  throughout the contract period.\\n\\n          42.6     Prevention of mosquito breeding\\\
      n\\n\\n\\n\\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 61\
      \ of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        50.15998077392578,\n        15.768376350402832,\n\
      \        564.21923828125,\n        24.210372924804688\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 0,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"                                                                                                     \
      \                              Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        85.5599594116211,\n        26.57955551147461,\n        88.32714080810547,\n        35.93199157714844\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 1,\n      \"text\": \" \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n      \
      \  85.5599594116211,\n        809.042724609375,\n        511.7470703125,\n        830.5714111328125\n    \
      \  ],\n      \"block_number\": 2,\n      \"text\": \"Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.               \
      \               September 2026         Section Page 61 of 114 \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n\
      \        85.55853271484375,\n        37.97945785522461,\n        520.3282470703125,\n        790.9714965820312\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 3,\n      \"text\": \"42.2 \\nOccupational Health Centre  \\n42.2.1 \\\
      nThe contractor shall ensure at a construction site an occupational health centre, \\nmobile or static is\
      \ provided and maintained in good order. Services and facilities \\nas per the scale lay down in Schedule\
      \ X of BOCWR. A construction medical \\nofficer appointed in an occupational health centre, possess the qualification\
      \ as \\nlaid down in Schedule XI of BOCWR. \\n \\n42.3 \\nAmbulance van and room \\n \\n42.3.1 \\nThe contractor\
      \ shall ensure at a construction site of a building or other \\nconstruction work that an ambulance van and\
      \ room are provided at such \\nconstruction site or an arrangement is made with a nearby hospital for providing\
      \ \\nsuch ambulance van for transportation of serious cases of accident or sickness \\nof workers to hospital\
      \ promptly and such ambulance van and room are \\nmaintained in good repair and is equipped with standard\
      \ facilities specified in \\nSchedule IV and Schedule V of BOCWR. \\n \\n42.4 \\nFirst-aid boxes \\n \\n42.4.1\
      \ \\nThe contractor shall ensure at a construction site one First-aid box for 100 workers \\nprovided and\
      \ maintained for providing First-aid to the building workers. Every First-aid \\nbox is distinctly marked\
      \ “First-aid” and is equipped with the articles specified in \\nSchedule III of BOCWR. \\n \\n42.5 \\nHIV/\
      \ AIDS prevention and control \\n \\n42.5.1 \\nThe contractor shall adopt the Employer’s Policy on “HIV /\
      \ AIDS Prevention and \\nControl for Workmen Engaged by Contractors” and the copy of the policy is given in\
      \ \\nAppendix No.: 4.  \\n \\n42.5.2 \\nThe Employer will engage a professional agency for implementing the\
      \ guidelines laid \\ndown in the policy and communicate to the contractor. \\n \\n42.5.3 \\nThe Contractor\
      \ shall extend necessary support to the appointed agency by deputing \\nthe workmen to attend the awareness\
      \ creation programmes. \\n \\n42.5.4 \\nThe contractor shall also extend necessary organizational support\
      \ to the appointed \\nagency for the effective implementation of the Employers’ workplace policy on \\nHIV/AIDS\
      \ for workmen of the Contractors. \\n \\n42.5.5 \\nAs laid down in the policy the contractor shall identify\
      \ peer educators (1 for every 100 \\nworkers) and refer them for professional training to the Employers’ appointed\
      \ agency \\nfor the purpose. \\n \\n42.5.6 \\nThe peer educators on completion of the training shall serve\
      \ as the focal point \\nfor any information, education and awareness campaign among the workmen \\nthroughout\
      \ the contract period. \\n \\n42.5.7 \\nThe peer educators will be paid a monthly honorarium as fixed by the\
      \ Employer for \\nrendering his services in addition to his regular duty. \\n \\n42.5.8 \\nThe total number\
      \ of peer educators (1 for 100 workers) shall always be maintained by \\nthe contractor. \\n \\n \\n42.5.9\
      \ \\nIn case if these peer educators leave the contractor by creating vacancy, then \\nthe contractor at his\
      \ own expense train the new replacement peer educator from \\nthe Employers’ appointed agency for the purpose.\
      \ \\n42.5.10 \\nIt is suggested to the contractor that due care should be taken to select the peer \\neducators\
      \ from among the group of workmen so that they remain with the contractor \\nthroughout the contract period.\
      \ \\n42.6 \\nPrevention of mosquito breeding \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        39.599998474121094,\n\
      \        24.479991912841797,\n        63.57600021362305,\n        41.38799285888672\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 4,\n      \"text\": \"249\\n\"\n    }\n  ]\n}"

  - id: Jd8ad6700267a399b
    okf_path: OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3.md
    okf_sha256: b973183de77a23a5c4e6f7df14a6bfe6e19a3add3e80a6dc1db238991aff8dd3
    heading: Clause 43.0 — Noise
    source_document: Vol3DLNP1.pdf
    source_sha256: 1ee3fc8dfd12f659f34446b0a6db74e1e1cdd4d593ca91b2a48d59bf1ef97d52
    pdf_pages:
    - 70
    sheet_cells: ''
    json_path: parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json
    json_sha256: dc172436c564fe7d5a449f7f786e6863b16e68ebd59020d555002ab6fc04758d
    json_pointer: /clauses/2/fragments/4
    json_record_id: null
    source_review_status: pending_manual_review
    quote: "{\n  \"pdf_page\": 70,\n  \"text\": \"ii)   Keep noise to the lowest reasonably practicable level. \
      \ Appropriate measures\\n                     will be taken to ensure that construction works will not cause\
      \ any unnecessary\\n                or excessive noise, which may disturb the occupants of any nearby dwellings,\\\
      n                schools, hospitals, or premises with similar sensitivity to noise. Use equipment\\n     \
      \           with  effective  noise-suppression  devices and employ  other  noise  control\\n             measures\
      \ as to protect the public.\\n\\n                  iii)   Schedule and conduct operations in a manner that\
      \ will minimize, to the greatest\\n                extent  feasible,  the  disturbance  to  the  public  in\
      \  areas  adjacent  to  the\\n                construction  activities and  to occupants  of  buildings  in\
      \ the  vicinity  of the\\n                construction activities.\\n\\n             iv)   The Contractor\
      \ shall submit to the Employer a Noise Monitoring and Control\\n              Plan (NMCP) under contract specific\
      \ Site Environmental Plan.   It shall include\\n                       full and comprehensive details of all\
      \ powered mechanical equipment, which he\\n              proposes to use during daytime and night - time,\
      \ and of his proposed working\\n             methods and noise level reduction measures. The NMCP shall include\
      \ detailed\\n               noise calculations and vibration levels to demonstrate the anticipated noise\\\
      n               generation and vibrations by the Contractor.\\n\\n           v)   The NMCP prepared by the\
      \ Contractor  shall guide the implementation  of\\n                construction  activity. The NMCP  will\
      \ be reviewed on a regular basis and\\n              updated  as  necessary  to  assure  that  current  construction\
      \  activities  are\\n               addressed.  It may appear as a regular agenda item in project coordination\\\
      n               meetings, if noise is an issue at any location in the contract.\\n\\n\\n43.4     Occupational\
      \ Noise\\n\\n\\n                i)     Protection  against  the  effects  of  occupational  noise exposure\
      \ should be\\n               provided when the sound level exceeds the threshold values as provided in\\n\
      \                Project SHE Manual.\\n\\n                  ii)   When employees are subjected to sound levels\
      \ exceeding those listed in the\\n                Table, feasible administrative or engineering controls should\
      \ be utilized as given\\n                   in this document and BMRC’s Project SHE Manual.\\n\\n        \
      \          iii)      If such controls fail to reduce sound levels within the levels of the table, personal\\\
      n                 protective equipment shall be provided and used to reduce sound levels within\\n       \
      \         the levels of the table.\\n\\n             iv)   When the daily noise exposure is composed of two\
      \ or more periods of noise\\n              exposure of different levels, their combined effect should be considered,\
      \ rather\\n               than the individual effect of each. Exposure to different levels for various periods\\\
      n                 of time shall be computed according to the formula and sample computations,\\n         \
      \     as given in project SHE Manual.\\n\\n\\n43.4      Vibration Level\\n\\n\\n43.4.1    In locations where\
      \ the alignment  is close to  historical  / heritage structures, the\\n          contractor shall prepare\
      \ a monitoring scheme prior to construction at such locations.\\n          This scheme for monitoring vibration\
      \ level at such historical / heritage sites shall be\\n         submitted to Employer for his approval. This\
      \ scheme shall include:\\n\\n                i)     Monitoring requirements  for  vibrations  at  regular\
      \  intervals  throughout  the\\n                construction period.\\n\\n                  ii)    Pre-construction\
      \  structural  integrity  inspections  of  historic  and  sensitive\\n                structures in project\
      \ activity.\\n\\n                  iii)    Information dissemination about the construction method, probable\
      \  effects,\\n                 quality control measures and precautions to be used.\\n\\n             iv)\
      \   The vibration level limits at work sites adjacent to the alignment shall conform to\\n               \
      \ the permitted values of peak p velocity as given in article project SHE Manual.\"\n}"

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"page_rect\": [\n\
      \    0.0,\n    0.0,\n    595.219970703125,\n    842.0\n  ],\n  \"pdf_page\": 66,\n  \"review_status\": \"\
      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n  \
      \                                                        250\\n42.6.1   Measures shall be taken to prevent\
      \ mosquito breeding at site. The measures to be\\n         taken shall include:\\n\\n\\n                i)\
      \    Empty cans, oil drums, packing and other receptacles, which may retain water\\n                  shall\
      \ be deposited at a central collection point and shall be removed from the\\n                   site regularly.\\\
      n\\n                  ii)      Still waters shall be treated at least once every week with oil in order to\
      \ prevent\\n              mosquito breeding.\\n\\n                  iii)    Contractor’s equipment and other\
      \ items on the site, which may retain water,\\n                  shall be stored, covered or treated in such\
      \ a manner that water could not be\\n                 retained.\\n\\n             iv)   Water storage tanks\
      \ shall be provided.\\n\\n\\n42.6.2    Posters in both Hindi, English and local language which draw attention\
      \ to the dangers\\n           of permitting mosquito breeding, shall be displayed prominently on the site.\\\
      n\\n\\n42.6.3   The contractor at periodic interval shall arrange to prevent mosquito breeding\\n        by\
      \ fumigation  / spraying of insecticides. Most  effective insecticides  shall\\n         include SOLFAC WP\
      \ 10 or Baytex, The Ideal Larvicide etc.\\n\\n\\n42.7     Alcohol and drugs\\n\\n\\n42.7.1   The contractor\
      \ shall ensure at  all times that no employee  is working under the\\n          influence of alcohol / drugs\
      \ which are punishable under Govt. regulations.\\n\\n\\n42.7.2   Smoking  at  public worksites by any employee\
      \  is also prohibited as per Govt.\\n          regulations.\\n\\n\\n43.0     Noise\\n\\n\\n43.1    The Contractor\
      \ shall consider noise as an environmental constraint in his design,\\n          planning and execution of\
      \ the Works and provide demonstrable evidence of the same\\n        on Employer’s request. The Contractor\
      \ shall, at his own expense, take all appropriate\\n        measures  to ensure  that work  carried out by\
      \ the Contractor and by  his sub-\\n          Contractors, whether on or off the Site, will not cause any\
      \ unnecessary or excessive\\n         noise which may disturb the occupants of any nearby dwellings, schools,\
      \ hospitals, or\\n         premises with similar sensitivity to noise.\\n\\n\\n43.1.1    Without prejudice\
      \ to the generality of the foregoing, noise level reduction measures\\n           shall include the following:\\\
      n\\n\\n                i)    The Contractor shall ensure that all powered mechanical equipment used in the\\\
      n             Works shall be effectively sound reduced using the most modern techniques\\n               \
      \ available including but not limited to silencers and mufflers.\\n\\n                  ii)   The Contractor\
      \ shall construct acoustic screens or enclosures around any parts\\n                 of the Works from which\
      \ excessive noise may be generated.\\n\\n\\n43.1.2   The Contractor shall ensure that noise generated by work\
      \ carried out by the Contractor\\n        and his sub-Contractors during daytime and night time shall not\
      \ exceed the maximum\\n          permissible noise limits, whether continuously or intermittently, as given\
      \ in the project\\n       SHE Manual. The same may be varied from time to time by and at the sole discretion\\\
      n           of the Employer, In the event of a breach of this requirement, the Contractor shall\\n       \
      \  immediately re-deploy or adjust the relevant equipment or take other appropriate\\n        measures to\
      \ reduce the noise levels and thereafter maintain them at levels which do\\n          not exceed the said\
      \ limits. Such measures may include without limitation the temporary\\n          or permanent cessation of\
      \ use of certain items of equipment.\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n\\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September\
      \ 2026     Section Page 62 of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        47.039981842041016,\n\
      \        15.768376350402832,\n        561.0993041992188,\n        24.210372924804688\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 0,\n      \"text\": \"                                                                                 \
      \                                                  Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual \\n\"\n    },\n\
      \    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.4399642944336,\n        26.57955551147461,\n        85.20714569091797,\n\
      \        35.93199157714844\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 1,\n      \"text\": \" \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n\
      \      \"bbox\": [\n        82.4399642944336,\n        809.042724609375,\n        508.6270751953125,\n   \
      \     830.5714111328125\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 2,\n      \"text\": \"Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation\
      \ Ltd.                              September 2026         Section Page 62 of 114 \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n\
      \      \"bbox\": [\n        82.4399642944336,\n        38.09945297241211,\n        512.7684326171875,\n  \
      \      573.4119262695312\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 3,\n      \"text\": \"42.6.1 \\nMeasures shall\
      \ be taken to prevent mosquito breeding at site. The measures to be \\ntaken shall include: \\n \\ni) \\nEmpty\
      \ cans, oil drums, packing and other receptacles, which may retain water \\nshall be deposited at a central\
      \ collection point and shall be removed from the \\nsite regularly. \\nii) \\nStill waters shall be treated\
      \ at least once every week with oil in order to prevent \\nmosquito breeding. \\niii) \\nContractor’s equipment\
      \ and other items on the site, which may retain water, \\nshall be stored, covered or treated in such a manner\
      \ that water could not be \\nretained. \\niv) \\nWater storage tanks shall be provided. \\n \\n42.6.2 \\nPosters\
      \ in both Hindi, English and local language which draw attention to the dangers \\nof permitting mosquito\
      \ breeding, shall be displayed prominently on the site. \\n \\n42.6.3 \\nThe contractor at periodic interval\
      \ shall arrange to prevent mosquito breeding \\nby fumigation / spraying of insecticides. Most effective insecticides\
      \ shall \\ninclude SOLFAC WP 10 or Baytex, The Ideal Larvicide etc. \\n \\n42.7 \\nAlcohol and drugs \\n \\\
      n42.7.1 \\nThe contractor shall ensure at all times that no employee is working under the \\ninfluence of\
      \ alcohol / drugs which are punishable under Govt. regulations. \\n \\n42.7.2 \\nSmoking at public worksites\
      \ by any employee is also prohibited as per Govt. \\nregulations. \\n \\n43.0 \\nNoise  \\n \\n43.1  \\nThe\
      \ Contractor shall consider noise as an environmental constraint in his design, \\nplanning and execution\
      \ of the Works and provide demonstrable evidence of the same \\non Employer’s request. The Contractor shall,\
      \ at his own expense, take all appropriate \\nmeasures to ensure that work carried out by the Contractor and\
      \ by his sub-\\nContractors, whether on or off the Site, will not cause any unnecessary or excessive \\nnoise\
      \ which may disturb the occupants of any nearby dwellings, schools, hospitals, or \\npremises with similar\
      \ sensitivity to noise. \\n \\n43.1.1  \\nWithout prejudice to the generality of the foregoing, noise level\
      \ reduction measures \\nshall include the following: \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n       \
      \ 82.43981170654297,\n        578.5794677734375,\n        517.2078247070312,\n        772.4918823242188\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 4,\n      \"text\": \"i) \\nThe Contractor shall ensure that all powered\
      \ mechanical equipment used in the \\nWorks shall be effectively sound reduced using the most modern techniques\
      \ \\navailable including but not limited to silencers and mufflers.  \\nii) \\nThe Contractor shall construct\
      \ acoustic screens or enclosures around any parts \\nof the Works from which excessive noise may be generated.\
      \ \\n \\n43.1.2  \\nThe Contractor shall ensure that noise generated by work carried out by the Contractor\
      \ \\nand his sub-Contractors during daytime and night time shall not exceed the maximum \\npermissible noise\
      \ limits, whether continuously or intermittently, as given in the project \\nSHE Manual.  The same may be\
      \ varied from time to time by and at the sole discretion \\nof the Employer, In the event of a breach of this\
      \ requirement, the Contractor shall \\nimmediately re-deploy or adjust the relevant equipment or take other\
      \ appropriate \\nmeasures to reduce the noise levels and thereafter maintain them at levels which do \\nnot\
      \ exceed the said limits. Such measures may include without limitation the temporary \\nor permanent cessation\
      \ of use of certain items of equipment. \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        546.0439453125,\n\
      \        24.479991912841797,\n        570.0199584960938,\n        41.38799285888672\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 5,\n      \"text\": \"250\\n\"\n    }\n  ]\n}"

    quote: "{\n  \"clause_id\": \"44.0\",\n  \"fragments\": [\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 70,\n      \"text\": \"\
      44.0      Ventilation and illumination\\n\\n\\n44.1      Ventilation\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\"\
      : 71,\n      \"text\": \"44.1.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction site of a building or other\
      \ construction\\n                 work that all working areas in a free tunnel are provided with ventilation\
      \ system as\\n                 approved by the DG and the fresh air supply in such tunnel is not less than\
      \ 6m3/min\\n                       for each building worker employed underground in such tunnel and the free\
      \ air flow\\n                movement inside such tunnel is not less than 9m/min.\\n\\n\\n          44.1.2\
      \   The oxygen level shall not be less than 19.5% in the working environment.\\n\\n\\n          44.2     \
      \ Illumination\\n\\n          44.2.1   The contractor shall take every effort to illuminate the work site\
      \ as per the Employer’s\\n                   requirement illustrated in general instruction BMRC/SHE/CEO/011.\\\
      n\\n          44.2.2   The contractor shall conduct a monthly illumination monitoring by lux meter for\\n\
      \                         all the locations and the report shall be sent to the Employer within 7th of the\\\
      n                  next month and the same shall be reviewed during the monthly SHE committee\\n         \
      \         meeting.\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"heading_line\": \"44.0      Ventilation and illumination\",\n  \"retained_lines\"\

  - id: Jeeb17043f0400cc9
    okf_path: OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3.md
    okf_sha256: b973183de77a23a5c4e6f7df14a6bfe6e19a3add3e80a6dc1db238991aff8dd3
    heading: Clause 46.0 — Welfare measures for workers
    source_document: Vol3DLNP1.pdf
    source_sha256: 1ee3fc8dfd12f659f34446b0a6db74e1e1cdd4d593ca91b2a48d59bf1ef97d52

    json_path: parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json
    json_sha256: dc172436c564fe7d5a449f7f786e6863b16e68ebd59020d555002ab6fc04758d

    quote: "{\n  \"clause_id\": \"46.0\",\n  \"fragments\": [\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 71,\n      \"text\": \"\
      46.0     Welfare measures for workers\\n\\n\\n          46.1      Latrine and Urinal Accommodation\\n\\n\\\
      n          46.1.1   The contractor shall provide one latrine seat for every 20 workers up to 100\\n      \
      \           workers and thereafter one for every additional 50 workers. In addition one\\n               \
      \     urinal accommodation shall be provided for every 100 workers.\\n\\n\\n          46.1.2   When women\
      \ are employed, separate latrine and urinals accommodation shall be\\n                   provided on the same\
      \ scale as mentioned above.\\n\\n\\n          46.1.3    Latrine and urinals shall be provided as per Section\
      \ 33 of BOCWA and maintained as\\n                   per Rule 243 of BOCWR and shall also comply with the\
      \ requirements of public health\\n                      authorities\\n\\n\\n          46.1.4   Moving sites\\\
      n\\n\\n          46.1.4.1  In case of works like track laying, the zone of work is constantly moving at elevated\\\
      n                      level or at underground level. In such cases mobile toilets with proper facility to\
      \ drain\\n                    the sullage shall be provided at reasonably accessible distance.\\n\\n\\n  \
      \        46.1.5    In case if the contractor fail to provide required number of urinals and latrines\\n  \
      \                or fail to maintain it as per the requirements of Public Health laws, the Employer\"\n  \
      \  },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"text\": \"shall have the right to provide/maintain through\
      \ renowned external agencies\\n           like “Sulabh” at the cost of the contractor.\\n\\n\\n46.2     Canteen\\\
      n\\n\\n46.2.1    In every workplace wherein not less than 250 workers are ordinarily employed\\n         the\
      \ contractor shall provide an adequate canteen conforming to Section 37 of\\n       BOCWA, Rule 244 of BOCWR\
      \ and as stipulated in Rule 247 of BOCWR the\\n        changes for food stuff shall be based on ‘no profit\
      \ no loss’ basis. The price list\\n          of all items shall be conspicuously displayed in such canteen.\\\
      n\\n\\n46.3     Serving of tea and snacks at the workplace\\n\\n\\n46.3.1   As per Rule 246 of BOCWR, at a\
      \ building or other construction work where a\\n        workplace is situated at a distance of more than 200\
      \ m from the canteen\\n         provided under Rule 244(1) of BOCWR, the contractor employing building works\\\
      n          shall make suitable arrangement for serving tea and light refreshment to such\\n         building\
      \ works at such place.\\n\\n46.4     Drinking water\\n\\n\\n46.4.1   As per Section 32 of BOCWA the contractor\
      \ shall make in every worksite,\\n          effective arrangements to provide sufficient supply of wholesome\
      \ drinking\\n         water with minimum quantity of 5 litres per workman per day. Quality of the\\n     \
      \    drinking water shall conform to the requirements of national standards on\\n         Public Health.\\\
      n\\n\\n46.4.2   While locating these drinking water facility due care shall be taken so that these are\\n\
      \           easily accessible within a distance of 200m from the place of work for all workers\\n        \
      \   at all location of work sites.\\n\\n\\n46.4.3     All such points shall be legible marked “Drinking Water”\
      \ in a language understood by a\\n          majority of the workmen employed in such place and such point\
      \ shall be situated\\n           within six metres of any washing places, urinals or latrines.\\n\\n\\n46.5\
      \     Labour Accommodation\\n\\n\\n46.5.1   The contractor shall provide free of charges as near as possible,\
      \ temporary\\n          living accommodation to all workers conforming to provisions of Section 34 of\\n \
      \      BOCWA. These accommodations shall have cooking place, bathing, washing\\n        and lavatory facilities\\\
      n\\n46.6     Creche\\n\\n46.6.1    In every workplace where in more than 50 female workers are ordinarily\
      \ employed,\\n          there shall be provided and maintained a suitable room for use of children under age\\\
      n           of 6 yrs, conforming to the provisions of Section 35 of BOCWA.\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"heading_line\"\
      : \"          46.0     Welfare measures for workers\",\n  \"retained_lines\": [\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\"\

      \    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 6,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n \
      \     \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 7,\n      \"text\": \"46.2     Canteen\"\n    },\n\
      \    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 8,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n \
      \     \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 9,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\"\
      : 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 10,\n      \"text\": \"46.2.1    In every workplace wherein not less\
      \ than 250 workers are ordinarily employed\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 11,\n      \"text\": \"         the contractor shall provide an adequate canteen conforming to Section 37\
      \ of\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 12,\n      \"text\": \"    \
      \   BOCWA, Rule 244 of BOCWR and as stipulated in Rule 247 of BOCWR the\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\"\
      : 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 13,\n      \"text\": \"        changes for food stuff shall be based\
      \ on ‘no profit no loss’ basis. The price list\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 14,\n      \"text\": \"          of all items shall be conspicuously displayed in such canteen.\"\n    },\n\
      \    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 15,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n\
      \      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 16,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"\
      pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 17,\n      \"text\": \"46.3     Serving of tea and snacks at\
      \ the workplace\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 18,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 19,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n\
      \    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 20,\n      \"text\": \"46.3.1   As\
      \ per Rule 246 of BOCWR, at a building or other construction work where a\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\"\
      : 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 21,\n      \"text\": \"        workplace is situated at a distance of\
      \ more than 200 m from the canteen\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 22,\n      \"text\": \"         provided under Rule 244(1) of BOCWR, the contractor employing building works\"\
      \n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 23,\n      \"text\": \"          shall\
      \ make suitable arrangement for serving tea and light refreshment to such\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\"\
      : 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 24,\n      \"text\": \"         building works at such place.\"\n   \
      \ },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 25,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n  \
      \  {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 26,\n      \"text\": \"46.4     Drinking water\"\
      \n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 27,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n\
      \    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 28,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n\
      \      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 29,\n      \"text\": \"46.4.1   As per Section 32\
      \ of BOCWA the contractor shall make in every worksite,\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"\
      source_line_number\": 30,\n      \"text\": \"          effective arrangements to provide sufficient supply\
      \ of wholesome drinking\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 31,\n   \
      \   \"text\": \"         water with minimum quantity of 5 litres per workman per day. Quality of the\"\n \
      \   },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 32,\n      \"text\": \"         drinking\
      \ water shall conform to the requirements of national standards on\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n\
      \      \"source_line_number\": 33,\n      \"text\": \"         Public Health.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\"\
      : 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 34,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n \
      \     \"source_line_number\": 35,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"\
      source_line_number\": 36,\n      \"text\": \"46.4.2   While locating these drinking water facility due care\
      \ shall be taken so that these are\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 37,\n      \"text\": \"           easily accessible within a distance of 200m from the place of work for\
      \ all workers\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 38,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"           at all location of work sites.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 39,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 40,\n \
      \     \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 41,\n      \"\
      text\": \"46.4.3     All such points shall be legible marked “Drinking Water” in a language understood by\
      \ a\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 42,\n      \"text\": \"     \
      \     majority of the workmen employed in such place and such point shall be situated\"\n    },\n    {\n \
      \     \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 43,\n      \"text\": \"           within six metres\
      \ of any washing places, urinals or latrines.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 44,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 45,\n \
      \     \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 46,\n      \"\
      text\": \"46.5     Labour Accommodation\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 47,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 48,\n \
      \     \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 49,\n      \"\
      text\": \"46.5.1   The contractor shall provide free of charges as near as possible, temporary\"\n    },\n\
      \    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 50,\n      \"text\": \"          living accommodation\
      \ to all workers conforming to provisions of Section 34 of\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n   \
      \   \"source_line_number\": 51,\n      \"text\": \"       BOCWA. These accommodations shall have cooking place,\
      \ bathing, washing\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 52,\n      \"\
      text\": \"        and lavatory facilities\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 53,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 54,\n \
      \     \"text\": \"46.6     Creche\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 55,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 56,\n \
      \     \"text\": \"46.6.1    In every workplace where in more than 50 female workers are ordinarily employed,\"\
      \n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 57,\n      \"text\": \"          there\
      \ shall be provided and maintained a suitable room for use of children under age\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"\
      pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 58,\n      \"text\": \"           of 6 yrs, conforming to the\
      \ provisions of Section 35 of BOCWA.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\"\
      : 59,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 60,\n \
      \     \"text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 61,\n      \"\
      text\": \"\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 62,\n      \"text\": \"\
      \"\n    },\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 72,\n      \"source_line_number\": 63,\n      \"text\": \"\"\n    }\n\
      \  ],\n  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\",\n  \"title\": \"Welfare measures for workers\"\n}"

    quote: "{\n  \"clause_id\": \"45.0\",\n  \"fragments\": [\n    {\n      \"pdf_page\": 71,\n      \"text\": \"\
      45.0     Radiation\\n\\n\\n          45.1    The use of radioactive substances and radiating apparatus shall\
      \ comply with the Govt.\\n                    regulatory requirements and all subsidiary legislation\\n\\\
      n\\n          45.2     Operations involving ionising radiation shall only be carried out after having been\\\
      n                  reviewed without objection by the Employers representative and shall be carried out in\\\
      n                  accordance with a method statement.\\n\\n\\n          45.3     each area containing irradiated\
      \ apparatus shall have warning notices and barriers, as\\n                    required by the Regulations,\
      \ conspicuously posted at or near the area.\\n\\n\\n          45.4     Radioactive substances will be stored,\
      \ used or disposed shall be strictly in accordance\\n                     with the Govt. Enactments.\\n\\\
      n\\n          45.5    The contractor shall ensure that all site personnel and members of the public are not\\\
      n                 exposed to radiation.\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"heading_line\": \"          45.0     Radiation\"\

- kind: parameter
  text: The contractor must arrange pre-employment medical examinations, post-illness or injury evaluations, and
    recurring medical checks once every two years up to age 40 and annually thereafter for drivers, transport equipment
    operators, and lifting appliance operators.

    source_document: Vol3DLNP1.pdf
    source_sha256: 1ee3fc8dfd12f659f34446b0a6db74e1e1cdd4d593ca91b2a48d59bf1ef97d52

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"page_rect\": [\n\
      \    0.0,\n    0.0,\n    595.219970703125,\n    842.0\n  ],\n  \"pdf_page\": 64,\n  \"review_status\": \"\
      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n  \
      \                                                        248\\n\\n               PART – III: OCCUPATIONAL\
      \ HEALTH AND WELFARE\\n\\n\\n41.0     Physical fitness of workmen\\n\\n\\n41.1    The contractor shall ensure\
      \ that his employees/workmen subject themselves to such\\n         medical examination as required under the\
      \ law or under the contract provision and\\n        keep a record of the same.\\n\\n\\n41.2    The contractor\
      \ shall not permit any employee/workmen to enter the work area under\\n          the influence of alcohol\
      \ or any drugs.\\n\\n\\n42.0     Medical Facilities\\n\\n\\n42.1     Medical Examination\\n\\n\\n42.1.1  \
      \ The contractor shall arrange a medical examination of all his employees including his\\n          sub-contractor\
      \ employees employed as drivers, operators of  lifting appliances and\\n          transport equipment before\
      \ employing, after illness or injury,  if  it appears that the\\n           illness or injury might have affected\
      \ his fitness and, thereafter, once in every two years\\n        up to the age of 40 and once in a year, thereafter.\\\
      n\\n\\n                i)    The Contractor shall maintain the confidential records of medical examination\
      \ or\\n                the physician authorized by the Employer.\\n\\n                  ii)   No building\
      \ or other construction worker is charged for the medical examination\\n             and the cost of such\
      \ examination is borne by contractor employing such building\\n               worker.\\n\\n              \
      \    iii)   The medical examination shall include: -\\n\\n\\n                a)     Full medical and occupational\
      \ history.\\n\\n                b)     Clinical examination with particular reference to\\n\\n           \
      \                         i)    General Physique;\\n\\n                                       ii)     Vision:-\
      \ Total visual performance using standard orthorator  like\\n                         Titmus  Vision  Tester\
      \  should be  estimated and  suitability  for\\n                         placement  ascertained  in accordance\
      \  with  the  prescribed  job\\n                           standards.\\n\\n                              \
      \          iii)    Hearing:  - Persons with normal must be able to hear a forced\\n                      \
      \    whisper at twenty-four feet. Persons using hearing aids must be\\n                           able to\
      \ hear a warning shout under noisy working conditions.\\n\\n                             iv)    Breathing:-\
      \ Peak flow rate using standard peak flow meter and the\\n                        average peak flow rate determined\
      \ out of these readings of the test\\n                          performed.  The  results  recorded  at  pre-placement\
      \  medical\\n                          examination could be used as a standard for the same individual at\\\
      n                           the same altitude for reference during subsequent examination.\\n\\n         \
      \               v)   Upper Limbs: - Adequate arm function and grip\\n\\n                             vi) \
      \   Spine: - Adequately flexible for the job concerned.\\n\\n                               vii)   Lower Limbs:\
      \ - Adequate leg and foot concerned.\\n\\n                                 viii)   General: - Mental alertness\
      \ and stability with good eye, hand and\\n                              foot coordination.\\n\\n         \
      \        c)   Any other tests which the examining doctor considers necessary\\n\\n\\n42.1.2.     If the contractor\
      \ fails to get the medical examination conducted as mentioned above,\\n          the employer will have the\
      \ right to get the same conducted by through an agency with\\n           intimation to the contractor and\
      \ deduct the cost and overhead charges.\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n\\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.           \
      \   September 2026     Section Page 60 of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n       \
      \ 47.039981842041016,\n        15.768376350402832,\n        561.0993041992188,\n        24.210372924804688\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 0,\n      \"text\": \"                                                \
      \                                                                                   Safety Health & Environment\
      \ (SHE) Manual \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.4399642944336,\n        26.57955551147461,\n\
      \        85.20714569091797,\n        35.93199157714844\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 1,\n      \"text\"\

      \   508.6270751953125,\n        830.5714111328125\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 2,\n      \"text\": \"\
      Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                              September 2026         Section Page 60\
      \ of 114 \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.4399642944336,\n        37.989418029785156,\n\
      \        85.20714569091797,\n        47.33189392089844\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 3,\n      \"text\"\
      : \" \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.43930053710938,\n        52.509437561035156,\n  \
      \      512.7670288085938,\n        331.01226806640625\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 4,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"PART – III: OCCUPATIONAL HEALTH AND WELFARE \\n \\n41.0  \\nPhysical fitness of workmen \\n \\n41.1  \\\
      nThe contractor shall ensure that his employees/workmen subject themselves to such \\nmedical examination\
      \ as required under the law or under the contract provision and \\nkeep a record of the same. \\n \\n41.2\
      \  \\nThe contractor shall not permit any employee/workmen to enter the work area under \\nthe influence of\
      \ alcohol or any drugs. \\n \\n42.0 \\nMedical Facilities \\n \\n42.1 \\nMedical Examination \\n \\n42.1.1\
      \  \\nThe contractor shall arrange a medical examination of all his employees including his \\nsub-contractor\
      \ employees employed as drivers, operators of lifting appliances and \\ntransport equipment before employing,\
      \ after illness or injury, if it appears that the \\nillness or injury might have affected his fitness and,\
      \ thereafter, once in every two years \\nup to the age of 40 and once in a year, thereafter.  \\n \\n\"\n\
      \    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        127.4378433227539,\n        336.0597839355469,\n        512.7656860351562,\n\
      \        423.5321350097656\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 5,\n      \"text\": \"i) \\nThe Contractor shall\
      \ maintain the confidential records of medical examination or \\nthe physician authorized by the Employer.\
      \ \\nii) \\nNo building or other construction worker is charged for the medical examination \\nand the cost\
      \ of such examination is borne by contractor employing such building \\nworker. \\niii) \\nThe medical examination\
      \ shall include: - \\n  \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.44039154052734,\n        428.5796813964844,\n\
      \        517.2083740234375,\n        778.971923828125\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 6,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"a) \\nFull medical and occupational history. \\nb) \\nClinical examination with particular reference to\
      \ \\ni) \\nGeneral Physique; \\nii) \\nVision:- Total visual performance using standard orthorator like \\\
      nTitmus Vision Tester should be estimated and suitability for \\nplacement ascertained in accordance with\
      \ the prescribed job \\nstandards. \\niii) \\nHearing: - Persons with normal must be able to hear a forced\
      \ \\nwhisper at twenty-four feet. Persons using hearing aids must be \\nable to hear a warning shout under\
      \ noisy working conditions. \\niv) \\nBreathing:- Peak flow rate using standard peak flow meter and the \\\
      naverage peak flow rate determined out of these readings of the test \\nperformed. The results recorded at\
      \ pre-placement medical \\nexamination could be used as a standard for the same individual at \\nthe same\
      \ altitude for reference during subsequent examination. \\nv) \\nUpper Limbs: - Adequate arm function and\
      \ grip \\nvi) \\nSpine: - Adequately flexible for the job concerned. \\nvii) \\nLower Limbs: - Adequate leg\
      \ and foot concerned. \\nviii) \\nGeneral: - Mental alertness and stability with good eye, hand and \\nfoot\
      \ coordination. \\nc) \\nAny other tests which the examining doctor considers necessary \\n \\n42.1.2. \\\
      nIf the contractor fails to get the medical examination conducted as mentioned above, \\nthe employer will\
      \ have the right to get the same conducted by through an agency with \\nintimation to the contractor and deduct\
      \ the cost and overhead charges. \\n \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        546.0439453125,\n\
      \        24.479991912841797,\n        570.0199584960938,\n        41.38799285888672\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 7,\n      \"text\": \"248\\n\"\n    }\n  ]\n}"

- kind: parameter
  text: First-aid boxes must be provided at a scale of one box for every 100 workers, clearly marked and equipped
    in accordance with Schedule III of BOCWR.
  evidence_ids:
  - J23ac14130beb4725
  relation_type: none
  explicitness: not_a_relation
  from_id: ''

    heading: 'Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare'
    source_document: Vol3DLNP1.pdf

    json_pointer: /source_page_context/1

    source_review_status: pending_manual_review
    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"page_rect\": [\n\
      \    0.0,\n    0.0,\n    595.219970703125,\n    842.0\n  ],\n  \"pdf_page\": 65,\n  \"review_status\": \"\
      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n249\\\
      n          42.2     Occupational Health Centre\\n\\n          42.2.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction\
      \ site an occupational health centre,\\n                 mobile or static is provided and maintained in good\
      \ order. Services and facilities\\n                as per the scale lay down in Schedule X of BOCWR. A construction\
      \ medical\\n                     officer appointed in an occupational health centre, possess the qualification\
      \ as\\n                     laid down in Schedule XI of BOCWR.\\n\\n\\n          42.3    Ambulance van and\
      \ room\\n\\n\\n          42.3.1   The contractor  shall ensure  at a construction  site of a building or other\\\
      n                  construction work that an ambulance van and room are provided at such\\n              \
      \    construction site or an arrangement is made with a nearby hospital for providing\\n                such\
      \ ambulance van for transportation of serious cases of accident or sickness\\n                   of workers\
      \  to  hospital promptly and such ambulance van and room are\\n                 maintained in good repair\
      \ and is equipped with standard facilities specified in\\n                Schedule IV and Schedule V of BOCWR.\\\
      n\\n\\n          42.4      First-aid boxes\\n\\n\\n          42.4.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction\
      \ site one First-aid box for 100 workers\\n                   provided and maintained for providing First-aid\
      \ to the building workers. Every First-aid\\n                 box  is  distinctly marked  “First-aid” and\
      \  is equipped with the  articles specified  in\\n                 Schedule III of BOCWR.\\n\\n\\n       \
      \   42.5     HIV/ AIDS prevention and control\\n\\n\\n          42.5.1   The contractor shall adopt the Employer’s\
      \ Policy on “HIV  / AIDS Prevention and\\n                    Control for Workmen Engaged by Contractors”\
      \ and the copy of the policy is given in\\n                Appendix No.: 4.\\n\\n          42.5.2   The Employer\
      \ will engage a professional agency for implementing the guidelines laid\\n               down in the policy\
      \ and communicate to the contractor.\\n\\n          42.5.3   The Contractor shall extend necessary support\
      \ to the appointed agency by deputing\\n                    the workmen to attend the awareness creation programmes.\\\
      n\\n          42.5.4   The contractor shall also extend necessary organizational support to the appointed\\\
      n                 agency  for the  effective implementation  of the Employers’ workplace  policy on\\n   \
      \              HIV/AIDS for workmen of the Contractors.\\n\\n          42.5.5   As laid down in the policy\
      \ the contractor shall identify peer educators (1 for every 100\\n                   workers) and refer them\
      \ for professional training to the Employers’ appointed agency\\n                       for the purpose.\\\
      n\\n          42.5.6   The peer educators on completion of the training shall serve as the focal point\\n\
      \                    for any information, education and awareness campaign among the workmen\\n          \
      \       throughout the contract period.\\n\\n          42.5.7   The peer educators will be paid a monthly\
      \ honorarium as fixed by the Employer for\\n                   rendering his services in addition to his regular\
      \ duty.\\n\\n          42.5.8   The total number of peer educators (1 for 100 workers) shall always be maintained\
      \ by\\n                    the contractor.\\n\\n          42.5.9    In case if these peer educators leave\
      \ the contractor by creating vacancy, then\\n                  the contractor at his own expense train the\
      \ new replacement peer educator from\\n                  the Employers’ appointed agency for the purpose.\\\
      n          42.5.10    It is suggested to the contractor that due care should be taken to select the peer\\\
      n                   educators from among the group of workmen so that they remain with the contractor\\n \
      \                  throughout the contract period.\\n\\n          42.6     Prevention of mosquito breeding\\\
      n\\n\\n\\n\\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 61\
      \ of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        50.15998077392578,\n        15.768376350402832,\n\
      \        564.21923828125,\n        24.210372924804688\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 0,\n      \"text\"\
