    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"page_rect\": [\n\
      \    0.0,\n    0.0,\n    595.219970703125,\n    842.0\n  ],\n  \"pdf_page\": 68,\n  \"review_status\": \"\
      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n  \
      \                                                        252\\n43.2.10  The Engineer shall monitor Contractor’s\
      \ performance of tasks specified, and  will\\n          inspect necessary records, reports and procedures\
      \ related to the control of noise.\\n\\n43.2.11  Impact monitoring shall be carried out at noise sensitive\
      \ receptor locations within 200\\n           feet of the construction site once each week and after a change\
      \ in construction\\n            activity. Construction noise measurements shall coincide with daytime and\
      \ nighttime\\n          periods of maximum noise generating construction activities.\\n\\n43.2.12  Noise Monitoring\
      \ data will be submitted in a Noise Measurement Report Form. It will\\n          contain the type of measurement,\
      \ duration of measurement, distance of monitoring\\n         from construction site, and construction equipment\
      \ working during monitoring period.\\n\\n43.2.13   appropriate  parameter  for measuring  construction  noise\
      \  impacts  shall be  the\\n          equivalent A-weighted sound pressure level (Leq) measured in decibels\
      \ (dB). The two\\n            statistical sound levels L10 and L90; the level exceeded for 10 and 90 percent\
      \ of the time\\n           respectively, shall also be recorded during monitoring. The L90 may be considered\
      \ as\\n          the ambient level into which the L10 as average peak level intrudes. The Lmax, Leq, L10\\\
      n        and L90 values will be reported in the noise measurement form along with allowable\\n         noise\
      \ limit. The duration of monitoring shall be for a minimum of 30 minutes.\\n\\n43.2..14  In no case  shall\
      \ the Contractor expose the public  to construction noise levels\\n         exceeding 90dBA (slow) or to impulsive\
      \ noise levels with a peak sound pressure level\\n         exceeding 140dB as measured on an impulse sound\
      \ level meter.\\n\\n43.2.15   Limit for construction noise is based on the existing ambient noise levels in\
      \ areas\\n          adjoining the construction sites.\\n\\n43.2.16 The noise levels emanating from any source\
      \ during construction, shall not exceed 5\\n         dB(A) or more above existing ambient pre-construction\
      \ noise levels when measured at\\n        a point outside the premises of the location of source. The same\
      \ may be varied from\\n         time to time by and at the sole discretion of the Engineer.\\n43.2.17  Where\
      \ there are no ambient noise measurements, the construction activities shall be\\n           limited to levels\
      \ measured at a distance of 200 feet from the construction limits or at\\n          the nearest affected building,\
      \ whichever is closer, as given in Table-2.\\n                                 TABLE-2\\n                \
      \  ALLOWABLE CONSTRUCTION NOISE\\n     LAND USE               MAXIMUM NOISE LEVELS- Lmax dB (A)\\n       \
      \                                     Day Time Night Time\\n          Residential                      75\
      \             65\\n          At all Times\\n          Commercial                                   85\\n \
      \            Industrial                                     90\\n\\n\\n43.2.18   At the surface of the construction\
      \ site during night time hours, the Contractor shall use\\n          only equipment that operating under full\
      \ load meets the noise limits specified in Table-\\n           3, if a sensitive receptor would be affected.\\\
      n                             TABLE-3\\n NOISE EMISSION LIMITS FOR CONSTRUCTION EQUIPMENT USED DURING NIGHTTIME\\\
      n              HOURS; MEASURED AT 50 FEET FROM CONSTRUCTION EQUIPMENT*\\n Equipment Category             \
      \                        Lmax Level dB(A)\\n\\n Backhoe                                                80\\\
      n Bar Bender                                              75\\n Chain Saw                                \
      \              81\\n Compactor                                              80\\n Compressor             \
      \                                80\\n Concrete Mixer                                           85\\n Concrete\
      \ Pump                                           82\\n Crane                                             \
      \     85\\n Dozer                                                  85\\n Front End Loader                \
      \                         80\\n Generator                                               82\\n Gradall    \
      \                                             85\\n Grader                                               \
      \   85\\n Paver                                                  85\\n\\n\\n\\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation\
      \ Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 64 of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        47.039981842041016,\n        15.768376350402832,\n        561.0993041992188,\n        24.210372924804688\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 0,\n      \"text\": \"                                                \
      \                                                                                   Safety Health & Environment\
      \ (SHE) Manual \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.4399642944336,\n        26.57955551147461,\n\
      \        85.20714569091797,\n        35.93199157714844\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 1,\n      \"text\"\
      : \" \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.4399642944336,\n        809.042724609375,\n     \
      \   508.6270751953125,\n        830.5714111328125\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 2,\n      \"text\": \"\
      Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                              September 2026         Section Page 64\
      \ of 114 \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.43956756591797,\n        38.09945297241211,\n\
      \        512.7686767578125,\n        421.1317443847656\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 3,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"43.2.10 \\nThe Engineer shall monitor Contractor’s performance of tasks specified, and will \\ninspect\
      \ necessary records, reports and procedures related to the control of noise. \\n \\n43.2.11 \\nImpact monitoring\
      \ shall be carried out at noise sensitive receptor locations within 200 \\nfeet of the construction site once\
      \ each week and after a change in construction \\nactivity. Construction noise measurements shall coincide\
      \ with daytime and nighttime \\nperiods of maximum noise generating construction activities. \\n \\n43.2.12\
      \ \\nNoise Monitoring data will be submitted in a Noise Measurement Report Form. It will \\ncontain the type\
      \ of measurement, duration of measurement, distance of monitoring \\nfrom construction site, and construction\
      \ equipment working during monitoring period. \\n \\n43.2.13    appropriate parameter for measuring construction\
      \ noise impacts shall be the \\nequivalent A-weighted sound pressure level (Leq) measured in decibels (dB).\
      \  The two \\nstatistical sound levels L10 and L90; the level exceeded for 10 and 90 percent of the time \\\
      nrespectively, shall also be recorded during monitoring.  The L90 may be considered as \\nthe ambient level\
      \ into which the L10 as average peak level intrudes. The Lmax, Leq, L10 \\nand L90 values will be reported\
      \ in the noise measurement form along with allowable \\nnoise limit.  The duration of monitoring shall be\
      \ for a minimum of 30 minutes.   \\n \\n43.2..14 In no case shall the Contractor expose the public to construction\
      \ noise levels \\nexceeding 90dBA (slow) or to impulsive noise levels with a peak sound pressure level \\\
      nexceeding 140dB as measured on an impulse sound level meter.   \\n \\n \\n43.2.15 \\nLimit for construction\
      \ noise is based on the existing ambient noise levels in areas \\nadjoining the construction sites. \\n \\\
      n43.2.16  The noise levels emanating from any source during construction, shall not exceed 5 \\ndB(A) or more\
      \ above existing ambient pre-construction noise levels when measured at \\na point outside the premises of\
      \ the location of source. The same may be varied from \\ntime to time by and at the sole discretion of the\
      \ Engineer.  \\n43.2.17   Where there are no ambient noise measurements, the construction activities shall\
      \ be \\nlimited to levels measured at a distance of 200 feet from the construction limits or at \\nthe nearest\
      \ affected building, whichever is closer, as given in Table-2.   \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n\
      \        82.43881225585938,\n        423.1892395019531,\n        512.7671508789062,\n        586.2517700195312\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 4,\n      \"text\": \"TABLE-2 \\nALLOWABLE CONSTRUCTION NOISE \\n \\nLAND\
      \ USE \\n \\n \\n   MAXIMUM NOISE LEVELS- Lmax dB (A) \\n                                                \
      \              Day Time Night Time \\n \\n    Residential \\n \\n \\n              75 \\n \\n    65 \\n  \
      \   At all Times \\n      Commercial                                         \\n \\n    85 \\n         \\\
      n      Industrial \\n \\n \\n \\n \\n \\n    90 \\n     \\n \\n43.2.18     At the surface of the construction\
      \ site during night time hours, the Contractor shall use \\nonly equipment that operating under full load\
      \ meets the noise limits specified in Table-\\n3, if a sensitive receptor would be affected. \\nTABLE-3 \\\
      nNOISE EMISSION LIMITS FOR CONSTRUCTION EQUIPMENT USED DURING   NIGHTTIME \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        87.83995819091797,\n        588.4292602539062,\n        508.44769287109375,\n        610.6116943359375\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 5,\n      \"text\": \"HOURS; MEASURED AT 50 FEET FROM CONSTRUCTION EQUIPMENT*\
      \ \\nEquipment Category\\nLmax Level dB(A)\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        87.83995819091797,\n\
      \        616.2592163085938,\n        403.2066955566406,\n        781.6114501953125\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 6,\n      \"text\": \"Backhoe \\n80 \\nBar Bender \\n75 \\nChain Saw \\n81 \\nCompactor \\n80 \\nCompressor\
      \  \\n80 \\nConcrete Mixer \\n85 \\nConcrete Pump \\n82 \\nCrane \\n85 \\nDozer \\n85 \\nFront End Loader\
      \ \\n80 \\nGenerator \\n82 \\nGradall \\n85 \\nGrader \\n85 \\nPaver \\n85 \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"\
      bbox\": [\n        546.0439453125,\n        24.479991912841797,\n        570.0199584960938,\n        41.38799285888672\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 7,\n      \"text\": \"252\\n\"\n    }\n  ]\n}"

      \        512.7686767578125,\n        421.1317443847656\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 3,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"43.2.10 \\nThe Engineer shall monitor Contractor’s performance of tasks specified, and will \\ninspect\
      \ necessary records, reports and procedures related to the control of noise. \\n \\n43.2.11 \\nImpact monitoring\
      \ shall be carried out at noise sensitive receptor locations within 200 \\nfeet of the construction site once\
      \ each week and after a change in construction \\nactivity. Construction noise measurements shall coincide\
      \ with daytime and nighttime \\nperiods of maximum noise generating construction activities. \\n \\n43.2.12\
      \ \\nNoise Monitoring data will be submitted in a Noise Measurement Report Form. It will \\ncontain the type\
      \ of measurement, duration of measurement, distance of monitoring \\nfrom construction site, and construction\
      \ equipment working during monitoring period. \\n \\n43.2.13    appropriate parameter for measuring construction\
      \ noise impacts shall be the \\nequivalent A-weighted sound pressure level (Leq) measured in decibels (dB).\
      \  The two \\nstatistical sound levels L10 and L90; the level exceeded for 10 and 90 percent of the time \\\
      nrespectively, shall also be recorded during monitoring.  The L90 may be considered as \\nthe ambient level\
      \ into which the L10 as average peak level intrudes. The Lmax, Leq, L10 \\nand L90 values will be reported\
      \ in the noise measurement form along with allowable \\nnoise limit.  The duration of monitoring shall be\
      \ for a minimum of 30 minutes.   \\n \\n43.2..14 In no case shall the Contractor expose the public to construction\
      \ noise levels \\nexceeding 90dBA (slow) or to impulsive noise levels with a peak sound pressure level \\\
      nexceeding 140dB as measured on an impulse sound level meter.   \\n \\n \\n43.2.15 \\nLimit for construction\
      \ noise is based on the existing ambient noise levels in areas \\nadjoining the construction sites. \\n \\\
      n43.2.16  The noise levels emanating from any source during construction, shall not exceed 5 \\ndB(A) or more\
      \ above existing ambient pre-construction noise levels when measured at \\na point outside the premises of\
      \ the location of source. The same may be varied from \\ntime to time by and at the sole discretion of the\
      \ Engineer.  \\n43.2.17   Where there are no ambient noise measurements, the construction activities shall\
      \ be \\nlimited to levels measured at a distance of 200 feet from the construction limits or at \\nthe nearest\
      \ affected building, whichever is closer, as given in Table-2.   \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n\
      \        82.43881225585938,\n        423.1892395019531,\n        512.7671508789062,\n        586.2517700195312\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 4,\n      \"text\": \"TABLE-2 \\nALLOWABLE CONSTRUCTION NOISE \\n \\nLAND\
      \ USE \\n \\n \\n   MAXIMUM NOISE LEVELS- Lmax dB (A) \\n                                                \
      \              Day Time Night Time \\n \\n    Residential \\n \\n \\n              75 \\n \\n    65 \\n  \
      \   At all Times \\n      Commercial                                         \\n \\n    85 \\n         \\\
      n      Industrial \\n \\n \\n \\n \\n \\n    90 \\n     \\n \\n43.2.18     At the surface of the construction\
      \ site during night time hours, the Contractor shall use \\nonly equipment that operating under full load\
      \ meets the noise limits specified in Table-\\n3, if a sensitive receptor would be affected. \\nTABLE-3 \\\
      nNOISE EMISSION LIMITS FOR CONSTRUCTION EQUIPMENT USED DURING   NIGHTTIME \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        87.83995819091797,\n        588.4292602539062,\n        508.44769287109375,\n        610.6116943359375\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 5,\n      \"text\": \"HOURS; MEASURED AT 50 FEET FROM CONSTRUCTION EQUIPMENT*\
      \ \\nEquipment Category\\nLmax Level dB(A)\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        87.83995819091797,\n\
      \        616.2592163085938,\n        403.2066955566406,\n        781.6114501953125\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\

      \ \\n80 \\nGenerator \\n82 \\nGradall \\n85 \\nGrader \\n85 \\nPaver \\n85 \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"\
      bbox\": [\n        546.0439453125,\n        24.479991912841797,\n        570.0199584960938,\n        41.38799285888672\n\

- kind: parameter
  text: Free tunnel working areas must be provided with an approved ventilation system supplying not less than 6
    cubic meters per minute of fresh air per underground worker and free air flow movement of not less than 9 meters
    per minute, with oxygen levels not less than 19.5 percent.
  evidence_ids:
  - J58e05cc1d406b16b
  relation_type: none
  explicitness: not_a_relation

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"page_rect\": [\n\
      \    0.0,\n    0.0,\n    595.219970703125,\n    842.0\n  ],\n  \"pdf_page\": 71,\n  \"review_status\": \"\
      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n255\\\
      n          44.1.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction site of a building or other construction\\\
      n                 work that all working areas in a free tunnel are provided with ventilation system as\\n\
      \                 approved by the DG and the fresh air supply in such tunnel is not less than 6m3/min\\n \
      \                      for each building worker employed underground in such tunnel and the free air flow\\\
      n                movement inside such tunnel is not less than 9m/min.\\n\\n\\n          44.1.2   The oxygen\
      \ level shall not be less than 19.5% in the working environment.\\n\\n\\n          44.2      Illumination\\\
      n\\n          44.2.1   The contractor shall take every effort to illuminate the work site as per the Employer’s\\\
      n                   requirement illustrated in general instruction BMRC/SHE/CEO/011.\\n\\n          44.2.2\
      \   The contractor shall conduct a monthly illumination monitoring by lux meter for\\n                   \
      \      all the locations and the report shall be sent to the Employer within 7th of the\\n               \
      \   next month and the same shall be reviewed during the monthly SHE committee\\n                  meeting.\\\
      n\\n\\n          45.0     Radiation\\n\\n\\n          45.1    The use of radioactive substances and radiating\
      \ apparatus shall comply with the Govt.\\n                    regulatory requirements and all subsidiary legislation\\\
      n\\n\\n          45.2     Operations involving ionising radiation shall only be carried out after having been\\\
      n                  reviewed without objection by the Employers representative and shall be carried out in\\\
      n                  accordance with a method statement.\\n\\n\\n          45.3     each area containing irradiated\
      \ apparatus shall have warning notices and barriers, as\\n                    required by the Regulations,\
      \ conspicuously posted at or near the area.\\n\\n\\n          45.4     Radioactive substances will be stored,\
      \ used or disposed shall be strictly in accordance\\n                     with the Govt. Enactments.\\n\\\
      n\\n          45.5    The contractor shall ensure that all site personnel and members of the public are not\\\
      n                 exposed to radiation.\\n\\n\\n          46.0     Welfare measures for workers\\n\\n\\n \
      \         46.1      Latrine and Urinal Accommodation\\n\\n\\n          46.1.1   The contractor shall provide\
      \ one latrine seat for every 20 workers up to 100\\n                 workers and thereafter one for every\
      \ additional 50 workers. In addition one\\n                    urinal accommodation shall be provided for\
      \ every 100 workers.\\n\\n\\n          46.1.2   When women are employed, separate latrine and urinals accommodation\
      \ shall be\\n                   provided on the same scale as mentioned above.\\n\\n\\n          46.1.3  \
      \  Latrine and urinals shall be provided as per Section 33 of BOCWA and maintained as\\n                 \
      \  per Rule 243 of BOCWR and shall also comply with the requirements of public health\\n                 \
      \     authorities\\n\\n\\n          46.1.4   Moving sites\\n\\n\\n          46.1.4.1  In case of works like\
      \ track laying, the zone of work is constantly moving at elevated\\n                      level or at underground\
      \ level. In such cases mobile toilets with proper facility to drain\\n                    the sullage shall\
      \ be provided at reasonably accessible distance.\\n\\n\\n          46.1.5    In case if the contractor fail\
      \ to provide required number of urinals and latrines\\n                  or fail to maintain it as per the\
      \ requirements of Public Health laws, the Employer\\n\\n\\n\\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.\
      \              September 2026     Section Page 67 of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\":\
      \ [\n        50.15998077392578,\n        15.768376350402832,\n        564.21923828125,\n        24.210372924804688\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 0,\n      \"text\": \"                                                \
      \                                                                                   Safety Health & Environment\
      \ (SHE) Manual \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.5599594116211,\n        26.57955551147461,\n\
      \        88.32714080810547,\n        35.93199157714844\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 1,\n      \"text\"\
      : \" \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.5599594116211,\n        809.042724609375,\n     \
      \   511.7470703125,\n        830.5714111328125\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 2,\n      \"text\": \"Bangalore\
      \ Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                              September 2026         Section Page 67 of 114 \\\
      n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.55896759033203,\n        38.09945297241211,\n      \
      \  515.8885498046875,\n        226.3718719482422\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 3,\n      \"text\": \"\
      44.1.1 \\nThe contractor shall ensure at a construction site of a building or other construction \\nwork that\
      \ all working areas in a free tunnel are provided with ventilation system as \\napproved by the DG and the\
      \ fresh air supply in such tunnel is not less than 6m3/min \\nfor each building worker employed underground\
      \ in such tunnel and the free air flow \\nmovement inside such tunnel is not less than 9m/min. \\n \\n44.1.2\
      \ \\nThe oxygen level shall not be less than 19.5% in the working environment. \\n \\n44.2  \\nIllumination\
      \ \\n44.2.1  \\nThe contractor shall take every effort to illuminate the work site as per the Employer’s \\\
      nrequirement illustrated in general instruction BMRC/SHE/CEO/011. \\n44.2.2 \\nThe contractor shall conduct\
      \ a monthly illumination monitoring by lux meter for \\nall the locations and the report shall be sent to\
      \ the Employer within 7th of the \\nnext month and the same shall be reviewed during the monthly SHE committee\
      \ \\nmeeting. \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.5599594116211,\n        231.02713012695312,\n\
      \        178.92880249023438,\n        253.01182556152344\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 4,\n      \"text\"\
      : \" \\n45.0 \\nRadiation \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.55854797363281,\n        257.77911376953125,\n\
      \        515.8870239257812,\n        492.6517639160156\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 5,\n      \"text\"\
      : \" \\n45.1 \\nThe use of radioactive substances and radiating apparatus shall comply with the Govt. \\nregulatory\
      \ requirements and all subsidiary legislation \\n \\n45.2 \\nOperations involving ionising radiation shall\
      \ only be carried out after having been \\nreviewed without objection by the Employers representative and\
      \ shall be carried out in \\naccordance with a method statement. \\n \\n45.3 \\neach area containing irradiated\
      \ apparatus shall have warning notices and barriers, as \\nrequired by the Regulations, conspicuously posted\
      \ at or near the area. \\n \\n45.4 \\nRadioactive substances will be stored, used or disposed shall be strictly\
      \ in accordance \\nwith the Govt. Enactments. \\n \\n45.5 \\nThe contractor shall ensure that all site personnel\

      \ \\n46.1 \\nLatrine and Urinal Accommodation \\n \\n46.1.1 \\nThe contractor shall provide one latrine seat\
      \ for every 20 workers up to 100 \\nworkers and thereafter one for every additional 50 workers. In addition\
      \ one \\nurinal accommodation shall be provided for every 100 workers. \\n \\n46.1.2 \\nWhen women are employed,\
      \ separate latrine and urinals accommodation shall be \\nprovided on the same scale as mentioned above. \\\
      n \\n46.1.3 \\nLatrine and urinals shall be provided as per Section 33 of BOCWA and maintained as \\nper Rule\

      \ \\n46.1.4.1 In case of works like track laying, the zone of work is constantly moving at elevated \\nlevel\
      \ or at underground level. In such cases mobile toilets with proper facility to drain \\nthe sullage shall\
      \ be provided at reasonably accessible distance. \\n \\n46.1.5 \\nIn case if the contractor fail to provide\
      \ required number of urinals and latrines \\nor fail to maintain it as per the requirements of Public Health\
      \ laws, the Employer \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        39.599998474121094,\n        24.479991912841797,\n\

      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n  \
      \                                                        256\\n          shall have the right to provide/maintain\
      \ through renowned external agencies\\n           like “Sulabh” at the cost of the contractor.\\n\\n\\n46.2\
      \     Canteen\\n\\n\\n46.2.1    In every workplace wherein not less than 250 workers are ordinarily employed\\\
      n         the contractor shall provide an adequate canteen conforming to Section 37 of\\n       BOCWA, Rule\
      \ 244 of BOCWR and as stipulated in Rule 247 of BOCWR the\\n        changes for food stuff shall be based\
      \ on ‘no profit no loss’ basis. The price list\\n          of all items shall be conspicuously displayed in\
      \ such canteen.\\n\\n\\n46.3     Serving of tea and snacks at the workplace\\n\\n\\n46.3.1   As per Rule 246\
      \ of BOCWR, at a building or other construction work where a\\n        workplace is situated at a distance\
      \ of more than 200 m from the canteen\\n         provided under Rule 244(1) of BOCWR, the contractor employing\
      \ building works\\n          shall make suitable arrangement for serving tea and light refreshment to such\\\
      n         building works at such place.\\n\\n46.4     Drinking water\\n\\n\\n46.4.1   As per Section 32 of\
      \ BOCWA the contractor shall make in every worksite,\\n          effective arrangements to provide sufficient\
      \ supply of wholesome drinking\\n         water with minimum quantity of 5 litres per workman per day. Quality\
      \ of the\\n         drinking water shall conform to the requirements of national standards on\\n         Public\
      \ Health.\\n\\n\\n46.4.2   While locating these drinking water facility due care shall be taken so that these\
      \ are\\n           easily accessible within a distance of 200m from the place of work for all workers\\n \
      \          at all location of work sites.\\n\\n\\n46.4.3     All such points shall be legible marked “Drinking\
      \ Water” in a language understood by a\\n          majority of the workmen employed in such place and such\
      \ point shall be situated\\n           within six metres of any washing places, urinals or latrines.\\n\\\
      n\\n46.5     Labour Accommodation\\n\\n\\n46.5.1   The contractor shall provide free of charges as near as\
      \ possible, temporary\\n          living accommodation to all workers conforming to provisions of Section\
      \ 34 of\\n       BOCWA. These accommodations shall have cooking place, bathing, washing\\n        and lavatory\
      \ facilities\\n\\n46.6     Creche\\n\\n46.6.1    In every workplace where in more than 50 female workers are\
      \ ordinarily employed,\\n          there shall be provided and maintained a suitable room for use of children\
      \ under age\\n           of 6 yrs, conforming to the provisions of Section 35 of BOCWA.\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n\\nBangalore\

    or contract and maintain corresponding records, while strictly prohibiting entry into work areas under the influence

# Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare Overview

> Draft model-derived knowledge. Citation validation establishes supplied-source provenance, not interpretation correctness.

## Summary

- Part III of the SHE Manual governs occupational health, medical examinations, occupational health centres, first-aid, HIV/AIDS prevention, mosquito breeding control, and noise/vibration limits for contractor personnel.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 64, 65, 66, 67, 68, 69, 70, 71, 72; quotation `S5b187b25e5ea2734`.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 42.0 — Medical Facilities|Clause 42.0 — Medical Facilities]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 64, 65, 66; quotation `J44c81c9cdd51865e`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/clauses/1`.

- Noise emission and control standards set daytime and nighttime limits, mandate Noise Monitoring and Control Plans (NMCP), and require mitigation for excessive construction noise.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 43.0 — Noise|Clause 43.0 — Noise]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 67; quotation `Jcf31dc552bb53faa`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/clauses/2/fragments/1`.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 43.0 — Noise|Clause 43.0 — Noise]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 68; quotation `J1dd8d9c8b2265f58`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/clauses/2/fragments/2`.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 43.0 — Noise|Clause 43.0 — Noise]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 69; quotation `J45fcc9b04b07ae52`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/clauses/2/fragments/3`.

- The Safety, Health and Environment Manual Part III establishes mandatory contractor requirements regarding physical fitness, medical examinations, occupational health centers, ambulance services, first aid, HIV/AIDS prevention, vector control, noise, ventilation, illumination, radiation, and worker welfare facilities.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 64; quotation `J4ba566627e05e212`. - The contractor must ensure that all employees and workmen undergo required medical examinations under law or contract and maintain corresponding records, while strictly prohibiting entry into work areas under the influence of alcohol or drugs.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 41.0 — Physical fitness of workmen|Clause 41.0 — Physical fitness of workmen]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 64; quotation `J26f0e77f41286863`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/clauses/0`.

## Key Parameters and Source Statements

- The contractor must arrange pre-employment, post-illness/injury, and periodic medical examinations (once every two years up to age 40 and annually thereafter) for all employees including drivers and equipment operators at the contractor's cost.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 42.0 — Medical Facilities|Clause 42.0 — Medical Facilities]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 64, 65, 66; quotation `J44c81c9cdd51865e`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/clauses/1`.

- Contractors must provide one First-aid box for every 100 workers, equipped in accordance with Schedule III of BOCWR.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 42.0 — Medical Facilities|Clause 42.0 — Medical Facilities]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 64, 65, 66; quotation `J44c81c9cdd51865e`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/clauses/1`.

- Contractors must identify and maintain peer educators at a ratio of 1 for every 100 workers for HIV/AIDS awareness and training.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 42.0 — Medical Facilities|Clause 42.0 — Medical Facilities]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 64, 65, 66; quotation `J44c81c9cdd51865e`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/clauses/1`.

- The contractor must arrange pre-employment medical examinations, post-illness or injury evaluations, and recurring medical checks once every two years up to age 40 and annually thereafter for drivers, transport equipment operators, and lifting appliance operators.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 64; quotation `J4ba566627e05e212`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/source_page_context/0`.

- First-aid boxes must be provided at a scale of one box for every 100 workers, clearly marked and equipped in accordance with Schedule III of BOCWR.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 65; quotation `J23ac14130beb4725`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/source_page_context/1`.

- Contractors must identify peer educators at a ratio of one for every 100 workers and support the employer's appointed agency for HIV/AIDS awareness and training.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 65; quotation `J23ac14130beb4725`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/source_page_context/1`.

- In free tunnels, fresh air supply must not be less than 6 m3/min per underground building worker, free air movement must be at least 9 m/min, and oxygen levels must not fall below 19.5%.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 44.0 — Ventilation and illumination|Clause 44.0 — Ventilation and illumination]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 70, 71; quotation `J79c6bd3eb032dd81`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/clauses/3`.

- Drinking water provisions require a minimum supply of 5 litres per workman per day, located within 200m of all work locations, and situated within six meters of washing places, urinals, or latrines.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 46.0 — Welfare measures for workers|Clause 46.0 — Welfare measures for workers]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 71, 72; quotation `Jeeb17043f0400cc9`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/clauses/5`.

- Latrine and urinal accommodations must be provided at a scale of one latrine seat for every 20 workers up to 100 workers, and one for every additional 50 workers thereafter, along with one urinal for every 100 workers.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 46.0 — Welfare measures for workers|Clause 46.0 — Welfare measures for workers]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 71, 72; quotation `Jeeb17043f0400cc9`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/clauses/5`.

- Construction noise limits for residential areas are 75 dB(A) during daytime and 65 dB(A) during nighttime, 85 dB(A) for commercial areas, and 90 dB(A) for industrial areas, measured at a distance of 200 feet from construction limits or the nearest affected building.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 68; quotation `J681b080009e1dad7`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/source_page_context/4`.

- The contractor shall not expose the public to construction noise levels exceeding 90 dBA slow or impulsive noise levels exceeding 140 dB peak sound pressure level.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 68; quotation `J681b080009e1dad7`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/source_page_context/4`.

- Construction vibration limits are set by aggregate duration: 0.01 in/sec for sustained 1 hour per day, 0.03 in/sec for transient under 1 hour per day, and 0.10 in/sec for transient under 10 minutes per day.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 69; quotation `Ja840a05f289c4458`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/source_page_context/5`.

- Free tunnel working areas must be provided with an approved ventilation system supplying not less than 6 cubic meters per minute of fresh air per underground worker and free air flow movement of not less than 9 meters per minute, with oxygen levels not less than 19.5 percent.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 71; quotation `J58e05cc1d406b16b`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/source_page_context/7`.

- Contractors must provide wholesome drinking water at a minimum quantity of 5 litres per workman per day, easily accessible within 200 meters from the place of work.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 72; quotation `J7eabb1054ab2b78e`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/source_page_context/8`.

- Latrine and urinal accommodations must be provided at a scale of one latrine seat for every 20 workers up to 100 workers and one for every additional 50 workers, plus one urinal accommodation for every 100 workers.
  - Evidence: [[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare]]; `Vol3DLNP1.pdf`; PDF page(s) 71; quotation `J58e05cc1d406b16b`. JSON: `parsed_json/vol3/part_3_health_welfare/health_welfare_clauses.json#/source_page_context/7`.

>       "text": "42.0     Medical Facilities\n\n\n42.1     Medical Examination\n\n\n42.1.1   The contractor shall arrange a medical examination of all his employees including his\n          sub-contractor employees employed as drivers, operators of  lifting appliances and\n          transport equipment before employing, after illness or injury,  if  it appears that the\n           illness or injury might have affected his fitness and, thereafter, once in every two years\n        up to the age of 40 and once in a year, thereafter.\n\n\n                i)    The Contractor shall maintain the confidential records of medical examination or\n                the physician authorized by the Employer.\n\n                  ii)   No building or other construction worker is charged for the medical examination\n             and the cost of such examination is borne by contractor employing such building\n               worker.\n\n                  iii)   The medical examination shall include: -\n\n\n                a)     Full medical and occupational history.\n\n                b)     Clinical examination with particular reference to\n\n                                    i)    General Physique;\n\n                                       ii)     Vision:- Total visual performance using standard orthorator  like\n                         Titmus  Vision  Tester  should be  estimated and  suitability  for\n                         placement  ascertained  in accordance  with  the  prescribed  job\n                           standards.\n\n                                        iii)    Hearing:  - Persons with normal must be able to hear a forced\n                          whisper at twenty-four feet. Persons using hearing aids must be\n                           able to hear a warning shout under noisy working conditions.\n\n                             iv)    Breathing:- Peak flow rate using standard peak flow meter and the\n                        average peak flow rate determined out of these readings of the test\n                          performed.  The  results  recorded  at  pre-placement  medical\n                          examination could be used as a standard for the same individual at\n                           the same altitude for reference during subsequent examination.\n\n                        v)   Upper Limbs: - Adequate arm function and grip\n\n                             vi)    Spine: - Adequately flexible for the job concerned.\n\n                               vii)   Lower Limbs: - Adequate leg and foot concerned.\n\n                                 viii)   General: - Mental alertness and stability with good eye, hand and\n                              foot coordination.\n\n                 c)   Any other tests which the examining doctor considers necessary\n\n\n42.1.2.     If the contractor fails to get the medical examination conducted as mentioned above,\n          the employer will have the right to get the same conducted by through an agency with\n           intimation to the contractor and deduct the cost and overhead charges."

>       "text": "42.2     Occupational Health Centre\n\n          42.2.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction site an occupational health centre,\n                 mobile or static is provided and maintained in good order. Services and facilities\n                as per the scale lay down in Schedule X of BOCWR. A construction medical\n                     officer appointed in an occupational health centre, possess the qualification as\n                     laid down in Schedule XI of BOCWR.\n\n\n          42.3    Ambulance van and room\n\n\n          42.3.1   The contractor  shall ensure  at a construction  site of a building or other\n                  construction work that an ambulance van and room are provided at such\n                  construction site or an arrangement is made with a nearby hospital for providing\n                such ambulance van for transportation of serious cases of accident or sickness\n                   of workers  to  hospital promptly and such ambulance van and room are\n                 maintained in good repair and is equipped with standard facilities specified in\n                Schedule IV and Schedule V of BOCWR.\n\n\n          42.4      First-aid boxes\n\n\n          42.4.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction site one First-aid box for 100 workers\n                   provided and maintained for providing First-aid to the building workers. Every First-aid\n                 box  is  distinctly marked  “First-aid” and  is equipped with the  articles specified  in\n                 Schedule III of BOCWR.\n\n\n          42.5     HIV/ AIDS prevention and control\n\n\n          42.5.1   The contractor shall adopt the Employer’s Policy on “HIV  / AIDS Prevention and\n                    Control for Workmen Engaged by Contractors” and the copy of the policy is given in\n                Appendix No.: 4.\n\n          42.5.2   The Employer will engage a professional agency for implementing the guidelines laid\n               down in the policy and communicate to the contractor.\n\n          42.5.3   The Contractor shall extend necessary support to the appointed agency by deputing\n                    the workmen to attend the awareness creation programmes.\n\n          42.5.4   The contractor shall also extend necessary organizational support to the appointed\n                 agency  for the  effective implementation  of the Employers’ workplace  policy on\n                 HIV/AIDS for workmen of the Contractors.\n\n          42.5.5   As laid down in the policy the contractor shall identify peer educators (1 for every 100\n                   workers) and refer them for professional training to the Employers’ appointed agency\n                       for the purpose.\n\n          42.5.6   The peer educators on completion of the training shall serve as the focal point\n                    for any information, education and awareness campaign among the workmen\n                 throughout the contract period.\n\n          42.5.7   The peer educators will be paid a monthly honorarium as fixed by the Employer for\n                   rendering his services in addition to his regular duty.\n\n          42.5.8   The total number of peer educators (1 for 100 workers) shall always be maintained by\n                    the contractor.\n\n          42.5.9    In case if these peer educators leave the contractor by creating vacancy, then\n                  the contractor at his own expense train the new replacement peer educator from\n                  the Employers’ appointed agency for the purpose.\n          42.5.10    It is suggested to the contractor that due care should be taken to select the peer\n                   educators from among the group of workmen so that they remain with the contractor\n                   throughout the contract period.\n\n          42.6     Prevention of mosquito breeding"
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 66,
>       "text": "42.6.1   Measures shall be taken to prevent mosquito breeding at site. The measures to be\n         taken shall include:\n\n\n                i)    Empty cans, oil drums, packing and other receptacles, which may retain water\n                  shall be deposited at a central collection point and shall be removed from the\n                   site regularly.\n\n                  ii)      Still waters shall be treated at least once every week with oil in order to prevent\n              mosquito breeding.\n\n                  iii)    Contractor’s equipment and other items on the site, which may retain water,\n                  shall be stored, covered or treated in such a manner that water could not be\n                 retained.\n\n             iv)   Water storage tanks shall be provided.\n\n\n42.6.2    Posters in both Hindi, English and local language which draw attention to the dangers\n           of permitting mosquito breeding, shall be displayed prominently on the site.\n\n\n42.6.3   The contractor at periodic interval shall arrange to prevent mosquito breeding\n        by fumigation  / spraying of insecticides. Most  effective insecticides  shall\n         include SOLFAC WP 10 or Baytex, The Ideal Larvicide etc.\n\n\n42.7     Alcohol and drugs\n\n\n42.7.1   The contractor shall ensure at  all times that no employee  is working under the\n          influence of alcohol / drugs which are punishable under Govt. regulations.\n\n\n42.7.2   Smoking  at  public worksites by any employee  is also prohibited as per Govt.\n          regulations."
>     }

### Jcf31dc552bb53faa

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 43.0 — Noise|Clause 43.0 — Noise]]

> {
>   "pdf_page": 67,
>   "text": "43.1.3   The noise monitoring requirements including monitoring locations are given in the\n                     project SHE Manual.\n\n\n          43.2    NOISE MONITORING\n\n          43.2.1   The activities which are expected to cause noise during the construction of Bangalore\n                   metro, include noise from construction equipment, construction activities such as portal\n                     construction, boring for piling, earthwork excavation, concreting, viaduct, station, depot\n                    construction (including shifting of launching truss  / girder) and removal of spoil and\n                movement of construction vehicles and delivery vehicles, traveling to and from the\n                    construction and disposal sites.\n          43.2.2   The level of impact of these noise sources depends upon the noise characteristics of\n                    the equipment and activities involved the construction schedule, and the distance from\n                   noise sensitive receptors.\n\n          43.2.3   The Noise Monitoring and Control Plan (NMCP) in contract specific site Environmental\n                    Quality Management Plan prepared by the Contractor shall establish procedures to\n                   monitor construction noise and determine when to apply measures to control noise\n                      pollution due to construction activities at work sites.\n\n          43.2.4   The NMCP will provide site description, define acceptable noise monitoring equipment,\n                    provide siting and operating procedures for noise equipment, indicate reports and record\n                   keeping on noise monitoring data.\n\n\n\n          43.2.5   The NMCP will provide guidance for construction activity.   It shall also address noise\n                  performance criteria used in the selection of construction equipment.\n\n          43.2.6   The Noise Monitoring and Control Plan shall provide for:\n                       a) Definition of noise-sensitive uses in the zones affected by construction.\n                       b)   Calculation of future noise levels at the closest noise-sensitive receptors to the\n                            construction activity based on construction activity and ambient noise levels.\n                        c)   Evaluation and specification of the noise abatement measures that can be\n                           applied to meet the noise objectives.\n                       d)   Monitoring construction activity and providing adjustments to noise abatement\n                             controls that may be required to increase their effectiveness.\n                       e)  regular reporting\n                                 f)   Requirements of NMCP:\n                                            (i)     It shall specify the nighttime and daytime construction activities, monitoring\n                                    locations, equipment, procedures, and schedule of measurements and\n                                   reporting methods to be used.\n                                                (ii)    It shall contain a scaled plan indicating monitoring locations, including\n                            measurements to be taken at construction site boundaries and at nearby\n                                     residential zones.\n                                                  (iii)  a record of the noise characteristics of powered mechanical equipment\n                             proposed to be used during day time and night time and of proposed\n                               working methods and of potential noise level reduction measures.\n                                      (iv)  Provisions for immediate notification when measured noise levels exceed\n                                 allowable limits,\n                                  (v)  Provide a reporting procedure whereby noise-monitoring data is furnished\n                                     to the Engineer on a weekly basis.\n\n          43.2.7    In defining the requirements of the NMCP, available measures for noise control, such\n                    as, the use of equipment with special exhaust silencers or enclosures, and the\n                    construction of temporary enclosures or noise barriers around specific construction\n                       site activity areas shall be considered.  It should also specify the measures to be\n                  adopted to counter the impact of noise pollution for public and workers working at site\n                    during construction.\n\n          43.2.8      If the measured noise levels exceed the noise limits, the noise levels shall be reduced\n                 by appropriate abatement measures.\n\n         43.2.9    The NMCP will be reviewed on a regular basis and updated as necessary to assure\n                    current construction activities are addressed."
> }

### J1dd8d9c8b2265f58

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 43.0 — Noise|Clause 43.0 — Noise]]

> {
>   "pdf_page": 68,
>   "text": "43.2.10  The Engineer shall monitor Contractor’s performance of tasks specified, and  will\n          inspect necessary records, reports and procedures related to the control of noise.\n\n43.2.11  Impact monitoring shall be carried out at noise sensitive receptor locations within 200\n           feet of the construction site once each week and after a change in construction\n            activity. Construction noise measurements shall coincide with daytime and nighttime\n          periods of maximum noise generating construction activities.\n\n43.2.12  Noise Monitoring data will be submitted in a Noise Measurement Report Form. It will\n          contain the type of measurement, duration of measurement, distance of monitoring\n         from construction site, and construction equipment working during monitoring period.\n\n43.2.13   appropriate  parameter  for measuring  construction  noise  impacts  shall be  the\n          equivalent A-weighted sound pressure level (Leq) measured in decibels (dB). The two\n            statistical sound levels L10 and L90; the level exceeded for 10 and 90 percent of the time\n           respectively, shall also be recorded during monitoring. The L90 may be considered as\n          the ambient level into which the L10 as average peak level intrudes. The Lmax, Leq, L10\n        and L90 values will be reported in the noise measurement form along with allowable\n         noise limit. The duration of monitoring shall be for a minimum of 30 minutes.\n\n43.2..14  In no case  shall the Contractor expose the public  to construction noise levels\n         exceeding 90dBA (slow) or to impulsive noise levels with a peak sound pressure level\n         exceeding 140dB as measured on an impulse sound level meter.\n\n43.2.15   Limit for construction noise is based on the existing ambient noise levels in areas\n          adjoining the construction sites.\n\n43.2.16 The noise levels emanating from any source during construction, shall not exceed 5\n         dB(A) or more above existing ambient pre-construction noise levels when measured at\n        a point outside the premises of the location of source. The same may be varied from\n         time to time by and at the sole discretion of the Engineer.\n43.2.17  Where there are no ambient noise measurements, the construction activities shall be\n           limited to levels measured at a distance of 200 feet from the construction limits or at\n          the nearest affected building, whichever is closer, as given in Table-2.\n                                 TABLE-2\n                  ALLOWABLE CONSTRUCTION NOISE\n     LAND USE               MAXIMUM NOISE LEVELS- Lmax dB (A)\n                                            Day Time Night Time\n          Residential                      75             65\n          At all Times\n          Commercial                                   85\n             Industrial                                     90\n\n\n43.2.18   At the surface of the construction site during night time hours, the Contractor shall use\n          only equipment that operating under full load meets the noise limits specified in Table-\n           3, if a sensitive receptor would be affected.\n                             TABLE-3\n NOISE EMISSION LIMITS FOR CONSTRUCTION EQUIPMENT USED DURING NIGHTTIME\n              HOURS; MEASURED AT 50 FEET FROM CONSTRUCTION EQUIPMENT*\n Equipment Category                                     Lmax Level dB(A)\n\n Backhoe                                                80\n Bar Bender                                              75\n Chain Saw                                              81\n Compactor                                              80\n Compressor                                             80\n Concrete Mixer                                           85\n Concrete Pump                                           82\n Crane                                                  85\n Dozer                                                  85\n Front End Loader                                         80\n Generator                                               82\n Gradall                                                 85\n Grader                                                  85\n Paver                                                  85"
> }

### J45fcc9b04b07ae52

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 43.0 — Noise|Clause 43.0 — Noise]]

> {
>   "pdf_page": 69,
>   "text": "Pneumatic Tools                                          85\n          Scraper                                                 85\n            Tractor                                                  84\n\n\n         Noise emission limits apply to equipment used at surface of the construction site during Night\n                    time hours of 9 p.m. to 6 a.m.\n\n      43.2.19     The adjustments for close in equipment noise measurement shall\n                 be made in accordance with Table-4.\n\n                                    TABLE – 4\n              ADJUSTMENTS FOR CLOSE-IN EQUIPMENT NOISE MEASUREMENTS\n                    (Measurement Values to be subtracted from Measured Sound)\n         Distance (Feet)                      Level to Estimate Sound Level at 50 Feet dB (A)\n              19-21                                               8\n              22-23                                               7\n              24-26                                               6\n              27-29                                               5\n              30-33                                               4\n              34-37                                               3\n              38-42                                               2\n              43-47                                               1\n              48-50                                               0\n\n                                       TABLE- 5.\n                          CONSTRUCTION VIBRATION LIMITS\n                             VIBRATION TYPE AND PERMISSIBL\n       AGGREGATE DURATION            LIMIT\n           Sustained (1 hr/day)                               0.01 in/sec (80 VdB re 10-6 in/sec)\n           Transient (<1 hr/day)                               0.03 in/sec (90 VdB re 10-6 in/sec)\n           Transient (<10 min/day)                            0.10 in/sec (100 VdB re 10-6 in/sec)\n\n\n          43.2.20  When  Diesel Generator (DG) Sets  are used  for  operation  of equipment and\n                   machinery, then ‘Standards and Guidelines  for  control  of Noise  Pollution from\n                    Stationery DG Sets’, under Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 shall apply.\n         43.2.21  Should the impact monitoring record noise levels which are:\n                         indicative of a deteriorating situation such that closer monitoring is reasonably\n                            indicated, or\n                        when in the opinion of the Engineer additional measurements are required in\n                        view of deteriorating noise environment, then, the Engineer may require the\n                         Contractor to increase the frequency of impact monitoring at any one or more of\n                          the monitoring stations until the results indicate an improving and acceptable\n                             level of noise.\n\n        43.2..22    The Contractor  shall submit a copy  of monitoring  results. The  results should\n                    represent  a statistical evaluation of data for evaluation of trends and comparison\n                      with noise emission standards.\n\n        43.2.23   Where the Engineer determines that the recorded Noise level is significantly\n                    greater than the acceptable levels, the Engineer may direct the Contractor to\n                   take effective remedial measures including, but not limited to, reviewing noise\n                  sources and modifying working procedures.\n\n         43.2.23    The Contractor shall inform the Engineer of all steps taken to investigate cause of\n                  exceedance and immediate action taken to avoid further exceedance through\n                       written reports and proposals for action under an Event Contingency Plan.\n\n          43.3       Control Requirements\n\n\n          43.3.1     Construction material should be operated and transported in such a manner as not to\n                     create unnecessary noise as outlined below:\n\n\n                                 i)    Perform Work within the procedures outlined herein and comply with applicable\n                        codes,  regulations, and  standards  established by  the  Central and  State\n                     Government and their agencies."
> }

### J4ba566627e05e212

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare]]

>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n                                                          248\n\n               PART – III: OCCUPATIONAL HEALTH AND WELFARE\n\n\n41.0     Physical fitness of workmen\n\n\n41.1    The contractor shall ensure that his employees/workmen subject themselves to such\n         medical examination as required under the law or under the contract provision and\n        keep a record of the same.\n\n\n41.2    The contractor shall not permit any employee/workmen to enter the work area under\n          the influence of alcohol or any drugs.\n\n\n42.0     Medical Facilities\n\n\n42.1     Medical Examination\n\n\n42.1.1   The contractor shall arrange a medical examination of all his employees including his\n          sub-contractor employees employed as drivers, operators of  lifting appliances and\n          transport equipment before employing, after illness or injury,  if  it appears that the\n           illness or injury might have affected his fitness and, thereafter, once in every two years\n        up to the age of 40 and once in a year, thereafter.\n\n\n                i)    The Contractor shall maintain the confidential records of medical examination or\n                the physician authorized by the Employer.\n\n                  ii)   No building or other construction worker is charged for the medical examination\n             and the cost of such examination is borne by contractor employing such building\n               worker.\n\n                  iii)   The medical examination shall include: -\n\n\n                a)     Full medical and occupational history.\n\n                b)     Clinical examination with particular reference to\n\n                                    i)    General Physique;\n\n                                       ii)     Vision:- Total visual performance using standard orthorator  like\n                         Titmus  Vision  Tester  should be  estimated and  suitability  for\n                         placement  ascertained  in accordance  with  the  prescribed  job\n                           standards.\n\n                                        iii)    Hearing:  - Persons with normal must be able to hear a forced\n                          whisper at twenty-four feet. Persons using hearing aids must be\n                           able to hear a warning shout under noisy working conditions.\n\n                             iv)    Breathing:- Peak flow rate using standard peak flow meter and the\n                        average peak flow rate determined out of these readings of the test\n                          performed.  The  results  recorded  at  pre-placement  medical\n                          examination could be used as a standard for the same individual at\n                           the same altitude for reference during subsequent examination.\n\n                        v)   Upper Limbs: - Adequate arm function and grip\n\n                             vi)    Spine: - Adequately flexible for the job concerned.\n\n                               vii)   Lower Limbs: - Adequate leg and foot concerned.\n\n                                 viii)   General: - Mental alertness and stability with good eye, hand and\n                              foot coordination.\n\n                 c)   Any other tests which the examining doctor considers necessary\n\n\n42.1.2.     If the contractor fails to get the medical examination conducted as mentioned above,\n          the employer will have the right to get the same conducted by through an agency with\n           intimation to the contractor and deduct the cost and overhead charges.\n\n\n\n\n\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 60 of 114",

### J23ac14130beb4725

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare]]

>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n249\n          42.2     Occupational Health Centre\n\n          42.2.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction site an occupational health centre,\n                 mobile or static is provided and maintained in good order. Services and facilities\n                as per the scale lay down in Schedule X of BOCWR. A construction medical\n                     officer appointed in an occupational health centre, possess the qualification as\n                     laid down in Schedule XI of BOCWR.\n\n\n          42.3    Ambulance van and room\n\n\n          42.3.1   The contractor  shall ensure  at a construction  site of a building or other\n                  construction work that an ambulance van and room are provided at such\n                  construction site or an arrangement is made with a nearby hospital for providing\n                such ambulance van for transportation of serious cases of accident or sickness\n                   of workers  to  hospital promptly and such ambulance van and room are\n                 maintained in good repair and is equipped with standard facilities specified in\n                Schedule IV and Schedule V of BOCWR.\n\n\n          42.4      First-aid boxes\n\n\n          42.4.1   The contractor shall ensure at a construction site one First-aid box for 100 workers\n                   provided and maintained for providing First-aid to the building workers. Every First-aid\n                 box  is  distinctly marked  “First-aid” and  is equipped with the  articles specified  in\n                 Schedule III of BOCWR.\n\n\n          42.5     HIV/ AIDS prevention and control\n\n\n          42.5.1   The contractor shall adopt the Employer’s Policy on “HIV  / AIDS Prevention and\n                    Control for Workmen Engaged by Contractors” and the copy of the policy is given in\n                Appendix No.: 4.\n\n          42.5.2   The Employer will engage a professional agency for implementing the guidelines laid\n               down in the policy and communicate to the contractor.\n\n          42.5.3   The Contractor shall extend necessary support to the appointed agency by deputing\n                    the workmen to attend the awareness creation programmes.\n\n          42.5.4   The contractor shall also extend necessary organizational support to the appointed\n                 agency  for the  effective implementation  of the Employers’ workplace  policy on\n                 HIV/AIDS for workmen of the Contractors.\n\n          42.5.5   As laid down in the policy the contractor shall identify peer educators (1 for every 100\n                   workers) and refer them for professional training to the Employers’ appointed agency\n                       for the purpose.\n\n          42.5.6   The peer educators on completion of the training shall serve as the focal point\n                    for any information, education and awareness campaign among the workmen\n                 throughout the contract period.\n\n          42.5.7   The peer educators will be paid a monthly honorarium as fixed by the Employer for\n                   rendering his services in addition to his regular duty.\n\n          42.5.8   The total number of peer educators (1 for 100 workers) shall always be maintained by\n                    the contractor.\n\n          42.5.9    In case if these peer educators leave the contractor by creating vacancy, then\n                  the contractor at his own expense train the new replacement peer educator from\n                  the Employers’ appointed agency for the purpose.\n          42.5.10    It is suggested to the contractor that due care should be taken to select the peer\n                   educators from among the group of workmen so that they remain with the contractor\n                   throughout the contract period.\n\n          42.6     Prevention of mosquito breeding\n\n\n\n\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 61 of 114",

>       "text": "42.2 \nOccupational Health Centre  \n42.2.1 \nThe contractor shall ensure at a construction site an occupational health centre, \nmobile or static is provided and maintained in good order. Services and facilities \nas per the scale lay down in Schedule X of BOCWR. A construction medical \nofficer appointed in an occupational health centre, possess the qualification as \nlaid down in Schedule XI of BOCWR. \n \n42.3 \nAmbulance van and room \n \n42.3.1 \nThe contractor shall ensure at a construction site of a building or other \nconstruction work that an ambulance van and room are provided at such \nconstruction site or an arrangement is made with a nearby hospital for providing \nsuch ambulance van for transportation of serious cases of accident or sickness \nof workers to hospital promptly and such ambulance van and room are \nmaintained in good repair and is equipped with standard facilities specified in \nSchedule IV and Schedule V of BOCWR. \n \n42.4 \nFirst-aid boxes \n \n42.4.1 \nThe contractor shall ensure at a construction site one First-aid box for 100 workers \nprovided and maintained for providing First-aid to the building workers. Every First-aid \nbox is distinctly marked “First-aid” and is equipped with the articles specified in \nSchedule III of BOCWR. \n \n42.5 \nHIV/ AIDS prevention and control \n \n42.5.1 \nThe contractor shall adopt the Employer’s Policy on “HIV / AIDS Prevention and \nControl for Workmen Engaged by Contractors” and the copy of the policy is given in \nAppendix No.: 4.  \n \n42.5.2 \nThe Employer will engage a professional agency for implementing the guidelines laid \ndown in the policy and communicate to the contractor. \n \n42.5.3 \nThe Contractor shall extend necessary support to the appointed agency by deputing \nthe workmen to attend the awareness creation programmes. \n \n42.5.4 \nThe contractor shall also extend necessary organizational support to the appointed \nagency for the effective implementation of the Employers’ workplace policy on \nHIV/AIDS for workmen of the Contractors. \n \n42.5.5 \nAs laid down in the policy the contractor shall identify peer educators (1 for every 100 \nworkers) and refer them for professional training to the Employers’ appointed agency \nfor the purpose. \n \n42.5.6 \nThe peer educators on completion of the training shall serve as the focal point \nfor any information, education and awareness campaign among the workmen \nthroughout the contract period. \n \n42.5.7 \nThe peer educators will be paid a monthly honorarium as fixed by the Employer for \nrendering his services in addition to his regular duty. \n \n42.5.8 \nThe total number of peer educators (1 for 100 workers) shall always be maintained by \nthe contractor. \n \n \n42.5.9 \nIn case if these peer educators leave the contractor by creating vacancy, then \nthe contractor at his own expense train the new replacement peer educator from \nthe Employers’ appointed agency for the purpose. \n42.5.10 \nIt is suggested to the contractor that due care should be taken to select the peer \neducators from among the group of workmen so that they remain with the contractor \nthroughout the contract period. \n42.6 \nPrevention of mosquito breeding \n \n"

### Jd8ad6700267a399b

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Clause 43.0 — Noise|Clause 43.0 — Noise]]

> {
>   "pdf_page": 70,
>   "text": "ii)   Keep noise to the lowest reasonably practicable level.  Appropriate measures\n                     will be taken to ensure that construction works will not cause any unnecessary\n                or excessive noise, which may disturb the occupants of any nearby dwellings,\n                schools, hospitals, or premises with similar sensitivity to noise. Use equipment\n                with  effective  noise-suppression  devices and employ  other  noise  control\n             measures as to protect the public.\n\n                  iii)   Schedule and conduct operations in a manner that will minimize, to the greatest\n                extent  feasible,  the  disturbance  to  the  public  in  areas  adjacent  to  the\n                construction  activities and  to occupants  of  buildings  in the  vicinity  of the\n                construction activities.\n\n             iv)   The Contractor shall submit to the Employer a Noise Monitoring and Control\n              Plan (NMCP) under contract specific Site Environmental Plan.   It shall include\n                       full and comprehensive details of all powered mechanical equipment, which he\n              proposes to use during daytime and night - time, and of his proposed working\n             methods and noise level reduction measures. The NMCP shall include detailed\n               noise calculations and vibration levels to demonstrate the anticipated noise\n               generation and vibrations by the Contractor.\n\n           v)   The NMCP prepared by the Contractor  shall guide the implementation  of\n                construction  activity. The NMCP  will be reviewed on a regular basis and\n              updated  as  necessary  to  assure  that  current  construction  activities  are\n               addressed.  It may appear as a regular agenda item in project coordination\n               meetings, if noise is an issue at any location in the contract.\n\n\n43.4     Occupational Noise\n\n\n                i)     Protection  against  the  effects  of  occupational  noise exposure should be\n               provided when the sound level exceeds the threshold values as provided in\n                Project SHE Manual.\n\n                  ii)   When employees are subjected to sound levels exceeding those listed in the\n                Table, feasible administrative or engineering controls should be utilized as given\n                   in this document and BMRC’s Project SHE Manual.\n\n                  iii)      If such controls fail to reduce sound levels within the levels of the table, personal\n                 protective equipment shall be provided and used to reduce sound levels within\n                the levels of the table.\n\n             iv)   When the daily noise exposure is composed of two or more periods of noise\n              exposure of different levels, their combined effect should be considered, rather\n               than the individual effect of each. Exposure to different levels for various periods\n                 of time shall be computed according to the formula and sample computations,\n              as given in project SHE Manual.\n\n\n43.4      Vibration Level\n\n\n43.4.1    In locations where the alignment  is close to  historical  / heritage structures, the\n          contractor shall prepare a monitoring scheme prior to construction at such locations.\n          This scheme for monitoring vibration level at such historical / heritage sites shall be\n         submitted to Employer for his approval. This scheme shall include:\n\n                i)     Monitoring requirements  for  vibrations  at  regular  intervals  throughout  the\n                construction period.\n\n                  ii)    Pre-construction  structural  integrity  inspections  of  historic  and  sensitive\n                structures in project activity.\n\n                  iii)    Information dissemination about the construction method, probable  effects,\n                 quality control measures and precautions to be used.\n\n             iv)   The vibration level limits at work sites adjacent to the alignment shall conform to\n                the permitted values of peak p velocity as given in article project SHE Manual."
> }

### Ja2ee2729a4e94860

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part3#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part III: Occupational Health and Welfare]]
